**670 insights** — atomic statements by experts, students and publications. Each carries who made it and where its evidence stands; see [[Evidence status]].

| [[+10% net gain set at concept stage]] | On Bestseller the team set an early-stage target of at least +10% biodiversity net gain, which then steered the strategy and concept phases. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | [[Biodiversity and ecological connectivity on logistics sites]], [[Biodiversity net gain]] |
| [[~20% cost premium, disclosed up front, not negotiated later]] | With prices fluctuating after the start of the war in Ukraine, biogenic construction looked ~20% more expensive. Because the ambition was fixed at the outset and the premium flagged early, the project avoided line-by-line bargaining. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Construction cost]] |
| [[0.5 kN per m² off the brief, and everything gets cheaper]] | On a London flagship tower, the design floor load would normally have been 3 kN/m²; they convinced the client to accept 2.5, which cascaded through the whole structure as cost and carbon savings. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Net Zero That Holds Up – Whole-Life Carbon, Design Choices, and Delivery]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Structural utilisation]], [[Construction cost]] |
| [[1,500 assets screened in a week — on five data fields]] | EY's in-house climate risk tool covers up to 2,000 companies/assets, with ~1,500 the largest job actually run, deliverable "within the week" given good data. The required data is minimal: building type, construction material, location, size, and a few more. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Beyond the Concrete – Designing Buildings That Last]] | — |
| [[1,500 lux is where people improvise blinds]] | Her own floor-measured metric uses a 1,500 lux maximum, a threshold derived not from theory but from studies observing the point at which office workers spontaneously start rigging up improvised shading. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Criteria for a Green Building – Beyond energy performance LEED - BREEAM - WELL]] | [[Worker comfort and well-being]], [[Daylight autonomy]] |
| [[1,500 people have touched data centres in one consultancy]] | Ramboll has roughly 1,500 staff with partial or full data centre experience; a concept design may take a handful of people, while detailed design scales rapidly across MEP, structures and architecture. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Cloud in the city – data centres, urban development and implications]] | — |
| [[1,900 heat inspections, zero citations]] | Around 1.8 million people work in US warehouses with heat-stress exposure, a workforce that nearly doubled between 2017 and 2022, in 20 billion sq ft of space with 30–40 ft ceilings. OSHA has run over 1,900 heat-related inspections since April 2022 — nearly tenfold the 2021 rate — and issued seven hazard alert letters to warehousing companies, with no citations or fines. Federal guidance puts heat-illness risk from strenuous work at 77 °F WBGT; workers at one air hub logged indoor temperatures up to 89 °F. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-external">External</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[NBC News investigation, 2023]] | [[Worker comfort and well-being]], [[Thermal comfort]] |
| [[10,000 m² of green roof, 300 trees, a cyclists' ramp]] | Roughly 10,000 m² of green roofs storing rainfall in soil rather than shedding it, more than 300 trees, and a separate access ramp built specifically so cyclists do not share the car route; the metro entrance and a small square were upgraded jointly with the City of Prague. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Criteria for a Green Building – Beyond energy performance LEED - BREEAM - WELL]] | [[Stormwater and sealed surfaces on logistics sites]], [[Green roof coverage]], [[Tree count and canopy cover]] |
| [[10% GWP cut from six combined measures]] | Campus Science Park in Brno, the first LEED v4 certified building in the country, achieved a 10% reduction in global warming potential — plus photochemical oxidants, acidification and non-renewable energy — across a complex of six calculated measures. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Criteria for a Green Building – Beyond energy performance LEED - BREEAM - WELL]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Whole-life carbon]] |
| [[13.5 Gt per yr, and where it actually sits]] | Buildings put at ~37% of global CO2, equal to about 13.5 Gt/yr; ~10 Gt operational in existing buildings, ~3.5 Gt/yr embodied, the vast majority in concrete, aluminium and steel. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-disputed">Disputed</span> | [[Net Zero That Holds Up – Whole-Life Carbon, Design Choices, and Delivery]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]] |
| [[159 kg per m² for under 1% of investment cost]] | On a Swedish project, a maximum embodied carbon value per square metre was written into the tender documents to the general contractor; over roughly 12 months of construction the team drove the result down to 159 kg/m², and the whole effort cost just under 1% of total investment. A recent Czech project (Ostrava) reached under 293 kg/m² — but without the tender requirement. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Model to Reality – Sustainability in Industrial Buildings]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Embodied carbon intensity]] |
| [[177 boreholes, 150 metres deep]] | Heating and cooling run on ground-source heat pumps served by 177 drilled cores 150 m deep; the system regenerates heat in the soil through the cooling season, and waste heat from cooling is recovered for domestic hot water. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Criteria for a Green Building – Beyond energy performance LEED - BREEAM - WELL]] | [[Operational energy and on-site renewables]] |
| [[20,000 factory visitors a year, aiming at 25,000]] | Czech open-house culture is strong: the existing cake factory receives around 20,000 visitors a year through glass panels and VIP walkthroughs; the new project is expected to triple that, and the brewery client is targeting ~25,000 a year. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Challenge the perception of industrial architecture as 'boxing']] | [[Public access and community use of logistics sites]], [[Social interaction index]] |
| [[2027–2030 – whole-life carbon becomes mandatory]] | Under the recast EPBD, member states must set GWP limits by 2027; public buildings must prove GWP from January 2028; from January 2030 all new buildings must demonstrate whole-life carbon assessment. Alongside EU taxonomy, described as a large problem for most EU countries. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-disputed">Disputed</span> | [[Logistics Development in the concept of certification and LCA]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Whole-life carbon]] |
| [[2050-proof is not enough; make it 2070-proof]] | The studio used to say all designs should be 2050 proof; it now says 2070, because the project lifespans they work with run 50 years and beyond. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Challenge the perception of industrial architecture as 'boxing']] | [[Adaptable and relocatable logistics structures]] |
| [[21% and 25% of the floor unusable — and legal]] | On a real Prague office floor plate, roughly 21% of the assessed area suffered excessive direct sunlight exposure and 25% excessive illuminance requiring occupants to shade — and that same floor plan would pass the Czech code without objection. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Criteria for a Green Building – Beyond energy performance LEED - BREEAM - WELL]] | [[Worker comfort and well-being]] |
| [[250 pages of German, six weeks to a masterplan]] | For the Max Planck Institute competition the team received ~3.5 GB / ~250 pages of technical German documentation with one German speaker on the team and six weeks to produce a concept. All PDFs were loaded into a secured NotebookLM space and queried conversationally, with citations back to source. The design director, who "only understands audio," was given an AI-generated podcast of the brief for the flight to Munich. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Henning Larsen Playbook for Biobased Buildings]] | — |
| [[27 neighbourhoods, 15,000 datasets, 1,200 answers]] | The study tested loneliness, house prices and visitor numbers across 27 Copenhagen neighbourhoods, combining statistics, web-scraped data, questionnaires, mobile geolocation and credit-card transaction flows. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Plot to Place – Designing Thriving and Resilient Neighborhoods]] | [[Social interaction index]] |
| [[297 hectares of green belt for one rail interchange]] | The West Midlands Interchange strategic rail freight interchange was approved in May 2020 over 297 hectares of designated green belt, against the local council's warning of "significant encroachment into the countryside" and its fear the rail link was a "Trojan Horse" for road-served warehousing. Approval was justified on GBP 680m/year of gross value added. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-external">External</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Planning Resource, 2020]] | [[Land take of logistics buildings]], [[Land take]] |
| [[35,000 m² of green façade, possibly the world's largest]] | The two high bays (roughly 140 x 120 m in plan, 35 m tall) carry close to 35,000 m² of green façade. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | [[Biodiversity and ecological connectivity on logistics sites]], [[Green plot ratio]] |
| [[350 people vs 6,000 next door]] | The neighbouring bol.com facility is comparable in size (~150,000 m²) but manual: reportedly ~6,000 staff against Bestseller's ~350 on its largest shift. Much of that workforce would be imported labour, and the operator reportedly sought temporary on-site housing — blocked because residential use is not permitted inside a logistics master plan. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | [[Automation and operational efficiency of logistics]], [[Level of automation]], [[Jobs created]] |
| [[355 GW sits on Europe's big roofs]] | The JRC's building-level model of all 271 million EU buildings puts total rooftop PV potential at ~2.3 TWp / ~2,750 TWh a year — 40% of electricity in a zero-emission 2050. Non-residential roofs alone hold ~500 GWp, and large commercial buildings over 2,000 m² account for about 355 GW: more than half the EU's entire 700 GW 2030 target, on the roofs of the building type this article is about. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-external">External</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[JRC - Nature Energy, 2026]] | [[Operational energy and on-site renewables]], [[Rooftop PV generation]] |
| [[36,000 lorry trips removed by barge]] | Franprix has supplied 300 Paris stores by river since 2012, running up to 42–48 containers a day from Bonneuil-sur-Marne to Quai de la Bourdonnais: 36,000 truck trips and 450,000 vehicle-km avoided over ten years, 250 tonnes of CO2 saved a year. Other Seine operators include Fludis (electric barge plus e-bikes) and a cable-car system that removed 10,000 trucks a month from one construction project. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-external">External</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[AIVP, Paris – transport on the River Seine]] | [[Last-mile delivery emissions and traffic]], [[Road freight impact]] |
| [[4.7% vs 8–9% vs 14–15%]] | Citing Eurostat, total built-up area at 4.7% of Czech territory against 8–9% in Germany and 14–15% in Benelux, arguing there is headroom before CZ reaches Western levels of land consumption. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-disputed">Disputed</span> | [[Industrial Architecture as Living Infrastructure – Why future design is defined by systems, data, and adaptability, not just form]] | [[Land take of logistics buildings]] |
| [[5% of Prague dug a metre deep]] | To make material extraction legible: 24 million cubic metres of stone, sand and brick were extracted over five years — equivalent to digging 5% of Prague to a depth of one metre. Prague's flushing and sink use runs to 12 billion litres a year, roughly a small dam or 5,000 Olympic pools. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Criteria for a Green Building – Beyond energy performance LEED - BREEAM - WELL]] | — |
| [[50 hours, 20 hours, 30 minutes]] | LCA from a bill of quantities takes roughly 50 hours and gives the best accuracy; a well-built BIM model (LOD 300 recommended) cuts that to around 20 hours; a simplified tool takes about 30 minutes with accuracy varying by roughly 10–20%. Garbage in a BIM model propagates straight into the result. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Logistics Development in the concept of certification and LCA]] | [[Embodied carbon intensity]] |
| [[50–80,000 people a day use their toilet]] | Their first Prague project was the toilets of a large shopping centre, used by 50–80,000 visitors per day at peak. Possibly the most-used architecture they will ever make, and entirely unattributed. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Build Your Own Playground – Architecture where no one looks for it]] | — |
| [[58% of the site lost, biodiversity still up 10–11%]] | Of nearly 300,000 m² of original landscape, only ~42% remains landscape after building, roofs and hardscape — yet the BNG comparison shows a ~10–11% increase, achieved through species variety rather than area. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | [[Biodiversity and ecological connectivity on logistics sites]], [[Biodiversity net gain]], [[Land take]] |
| [[6,500 mayors, none of them motivated]] | ~6,500 municipalities, most under 1,000 inhabitants, each with its own master plan. Development brings extra work, pressure on schools and local opposition; the central grant arrives regardless. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Industrial Architecture as Living Infrastructure – Why future design is defined by systems, data, and adaptability, not just form]] | — |
| [[7,000 m² of farming on a Paris data centre roof]] | Equinix, a colocation provider, built a data centre in northern Paris with a greenhouse urban farm on top, heated by the building's waste heat and producing vegetables and fruit — nearly 7,000 square metres of farming. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Cloud in the city – data centres, urban development and implications]] | [[Waste heat and cooling of data centres]], [[Waste heat recovery]], [[Katrin Rybinskiy]] |
| [[78 of 87 assets already at high drought risk]] | From an anonymised client portfolio: under the shown scenario/horizon (RCP 4.5, 2050), roughly 78 of 87 assets sit at high or very high drought risk *today*, with no change into the future; for temperature, 43 assets move into very high risk. Some risk is not future — it is already baseline. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Beyond the Concrete – Designing Buildings That Last]] | — |
| [[78% of land already altered]] | Humans have cultivated, exploited, drained or coated 78% of land globally, leaving very little real nature — framed as the root of both the climate and biodiversity crises. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-disputed">Disputed</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | — |
| [[8–10% of the area, 50% of the people]] | Only ~8–10% of the 155,000 m² is administration area, yet staffing between automated hall and offices is roughly equal. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | [[Automation and operational efficiency of logistics]] |
| [[80–90% of network designs never reach the market]] | Per his network-design partners, the large majority of commissioned supply-chain optimisation studies die at costing, exactly as architectural concepts do. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Why CEE is the Hottest Location in Europe for supply chain, logistics and industrial Development]] | — |
| [[80% of the market need, not 100% of one tenant]] | The investor horizon is 15–20 years while leases run 5–10; a building must fit roughly 80% of market demand so tenants can be swapped without touching the asset's fundamentals. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Industrial Architecture as Living Infrastructure – Why future design is defined by systems, data, and adaptability, not just form]] | [[Adaptable and relocatable logistics structures]], [[Adaptability]] |
| [[80% of what a city needs is made outside it]] | Urbanism's problem is that most of what a city consumes is produced elsewhere, so out-of-town facilities should combine nature outside with production inside and serve the city directly. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Plot to Place – Designing Thriving and Resilient Neighborhoods]] | — |
| [[85 GW blocked by roofs that can't take the weight]] | A Becquerel Institute analysis finds more than 85 GW of European rooftop potential — 38 GW industrial, 48 GW commercial — is unreachable with conventional modules because the roofs cannot carry over 10 kg/m² without reinforcement. Lightweight modules at least 50% lighter would unlock it. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-external">External</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Becquerel Institute, via pv magazine, March 2026]] | [[Operational energy and on-site renewables]], [[Structural utilisation]], [[Rooftop PV generation]] |
| [[A 1 – 18 contrast ratio gives you migraines]] | With a monitor fixed at 300 cd/m², immediately adjacent surfaces can exceed 6,000 cd/m², and even distant surfaces such as the facade or window leave a ratio of about 1:18 — high enough to be linked to migraines, poor concentration and lost productivity. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Criteria for a Green Building – Beyond energy performance LEED - BREEAM - WELL]] | [[Worker comfort and well-being]] |
| [[A baseline they never intended to build]] | Certain from the start they would not build in concrete and steel, the team still calculated the full carbon cost of a conventional structure purely as a scoring baseline — yielding a claimed ~30–37% reduction on structural decisions alone. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Embodied carbon intensity]] |
| [[A bird island as design precedent]] | The site sits on the Flevopolder, entirely reclaimed land with very low biodiversity. Offshore lies a second reclaimed-land project built purely to attract migrating birds — that precedent triggered the idea that a logistics site's leftover land could be nature rather than mown lawn. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | [[Biodiversity and ecological connectivity on logistics sites]] |
| [[A blank page as a portfolio slide]] | To make the point about anonymous practice, they show an empty slide. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Build Your Own Playground – Architecture where no one looks for it]] | — |
| [[A brick shortage, not ESG, opened the door]] | The pitch that got CLT accepted was a 2021 brick shortage and price spike, not carbon. At the time there was no discussion of carbon footprint at all with the client; the ESG framing arrived only afterwards. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Build Your Own Playground – Architecture where no one looks for it]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]] |
| [[A company that owns nothing but its people's time]] | Illustrating HR risk: EY globally has over 400,000 colleagues but the firm itself "does not own anything, just owns our time" — so any acquirer's due diligence would be about whether the staff are happy and staying. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Beyond the Concrete – Designing Buildings That Last]] | — |
| [[A dead steel mill is infrastructure]] | A former steel mill already had 30 MW of power, 1,500 truck movements a day and 6,000 workers arriving. The problem is that Czech zoning does not guarantee you inherit it, and the surrounding city consumed the road, sewage and electrical capacity in the intervening decade. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Industrial Architecture as Living Infrastructure – Why future design is defined by systems, data, and adaptability, not just form]] | — |
| [[A dump site that forbade digging became islands]] | On an 18-hectare former dump, piling, excavation and parking basements were all impossible; the constraint became the concept — neighbourhoods raised as real islands ~2 m above a central wetland that absorbs stormwater, with parking pushed to garages on a peripheral ring road. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Plot to Place – Designing Thriving and Resilient Neighborhoods]] | [[Stormwater and sealed surfaces on logistics sites]], [[Stormwater retention volume]] |
| [[A forgotten typology that used to be the highlight]] | Most architects consider warehouses and factories ugly and boring, and the public never wants to visit one — yet in an earlier era the factory was the highlight of architecture and urbanism, an open, inviting area contributing to the city economically and socially. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Challenge the perception of industrial architecture as 'boxing']] | — |
| [[A green roof without maintenance turns grey again]] | Roofing a whole warehouse in green scores well and addresses heat island, but unmaintained green roofs die. The maintenance regime — who will care for it — must be designed alongside it. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Logistics Development in the concept of certification and LCA]] | [[Biodiversity and ecological connectivity on logistics sites]], [[Green roof coverage]] |
| [[A greenwash premium of about 10%]] | There are companies charging roughly 10% more for materials branded "green something or other" that are neither clever nor particularly low carbon. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Net Zero That Holds Up – Whole-Life Carbon, Design Choices, and Delivery]] | — |
| [[A heart that does not stop beating]] | The pandemic proved logistics is non-substitutable: retail can move online and offices home for a year, but stopping a fulfilment centre or a pharma flow is immediate systemic disruption. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Industrial Architecture as Living Infrastructure – Why future design is defined by systems, data, and adaptability, not just form]] | — |
| [[A High Line for a whole industrial estate]] | At Volvo's Lundby campus — a fenced, active R&D and production site the city has grown around — the solution is a skywalk layer using the hilly topography to give the public and soft mobility access over the site. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Plot to Place – Designing Thriving and Resilient Neighborhoods]] | [[Public access and community use of logistics sites]], [[Urban integration of logistics buildings]], [[Ilinca Maria Baciu]] |
| [[A logistics hotel with football pitches on top]] | Sogaris' Chapelle International, Paris (A26 architectes, 2018), stacks 41,500 m² of programme on a former rail freight yard: 15,000 m² of ground-floor urban logistics designed with capacity for internalised rail freight, offices above, and a roof carrying sports pitches, an urban farm, a restaurant and a fitness club, with a district heating loop fed by waste heat from a data centre. Eleven tenants, around 350 jobs; MIPIM 2018 award for best industrial and logistics development. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-external">External</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Sogaris, Chapelle International]] | [[Urban integration of logistics buildings]], [[Public access and community use of logistics sites]], [[Mixed-use ratio]] |
| [[A logistics-specific method would start from territory]] | An ideal methodology for logistics would reflect the scale, focus on territorial impact, evaluate impacts across categories, and centre transport — named as the biggest problem of this building type — rather than reusing a generic building framework. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Logistics Development in the concept of certification and LCA]] | — |
| [[A low-carbon warehouse can win architecture awards]] | His closing line, against the assumption that logistics sheds sit outside architectural ambition. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Net Zero That Holds Up – Whole-Life Carbon, Design Choices, and Delivery]] | — |
| [[A masterplan brief built from scraped Instagram]] | For an industrial harbour competition in Copenhagen the team scraped social media imagery of the site, classified content with Clarifai (trees, bikes, lanes, buildings) and clustered sentiment and time-of-day to derive planning recommendations. They flag the bias risk explicitly: if the data is generated by whoever happens to be on site, you design for those people. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Henning Larsen Playbook for Biobased Buildings]] | — |
| [[A physical sandbox for landscape design]] | The landscape team uses an augmented-reality sandbox with a depth sensor that registers topography live and links back to the Rhino model for terrain, water runoff and soil-balance calculations. Big developers play with it as readily as visiting children do. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Henning Larsen Playbook for Biobased Buildings]] | — |
| [[A portfolio's climate exposure priced at ~EUR 30 million]] | The same anonymised assessment translated physical risk into money: the portfolio is or will be affected to the tune of about 30 million euros in required investment or financial losses. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Beyond the Concrete – Designing Buildings That Last]] | — |
| [[A quarter of the office is landscape]] | Roughly 1/4 of Henning Larsen's staff are landscape architects, brought in as co-authors from project start; Meisel oversees around 150 landscape designers within Ramboll. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | — |
| [[A quarter of the reference building's energy cost]] | The ČSOB SHQ energy model comes out at about one quarter of the LEED reference case — measured, as LEED requires, in energy cost rather than in technical consumption units. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Criteria for a Green Building – Beyond energy performance LEED - BREEAM - WELL]] | [[Operational energy and on-site renewables]] |
| [[A rain garden answers four risks at once]] | A single adaptation measure — creating a rain garden — is credited against heat, geotechnical drought, floods, and human health and well-being simultaneously. The tool's measure library is recommended as design inspiration. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Beyond the Concrete – Designing Buildings That Last]] | [[Stormwater and sealed surfaces on logistics sites]], [[Outdoor microclimate around logistics buildings]] |
| [[A reflective roof is a public health measure]] | Cool roofs cut maximum indoor temperatures by 1.2–3.3 °C in unconditioned buildings and reduce peak cooling demand by 11–27% in air-conditioned ones. A UK study found city-wide cool roofs could offset 18% of heat-related deaths attributable to the urban heat island. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-external">External</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[US EPA, Using Cool Roofs to Reduce Heat Islands]] | [[Worker comfort and well-being]], [[Urban heat island effect]], [[Thermal comfort]] |
| [[A room becomes a bicycle facility]] | Where floor area happens to be spare, it is easy to designate it a bicycle facility and collect the credit; where there is no spare space, the same provision is almost impossible to insert. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Criteria for a Green Building – Beyond energy performance LEED - BREEAM - WELL]] | — |
| [[A rule you cannot enforce is not a rule]] | every rule is great, but what do you do if you can't actually enforce it and there's no control around | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Integrated Urban Logistics – From last-mile to mobility hubs]] | — |
| [[A supplier who had never built at this scale]] | The straw supplier had never delivered a project of this size, so the team carried a palette of Plan-B specifications for every ambitious choice — fallbacks pre-agreed to survive fire, humidity or cost objections without changing the design fundamentally. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | — |
| [[A swimming pool heated by servers in Milan]] | Ramboll is working on a project in Milan pairing a small data centre with a swimming pool heated by its waste heat — his illustration that the type can be small and connected rather than a fortress. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Cloud in the city – data centres, urban development and implications]] | [[Waste heat and cooling of data centres]], [[Julia Godard Lombard]] |
| [[A system with a data centre at its heart, not a standalone asset]] | His key message: treat the facility as a node in an energy, utility and urban system — how power is produced and distributed, how heat and waste are used, how it sits in city and landscape. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Cloud in the city – data centres, urban development and implications]] | [[Waste heat and cooling of data centres]], [[Urban integration of logistics buildings]] |
| [[A three-dimensional street inside the shed]] | Rather than splitting the building into blue- and white-collar halves, all shared functions sit in a "three-dimensional street" in the overlap zone, so all staff across three shifts pass each other. Explicitly likened to a city street. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | [[Worker comfort and well-being]] |
| [[A translucent factory is a light-pollution machine]] | If you run a 24/7 factory behind a translucent envelope, the building glows: light pollution becomes a design problem. One answer is not lighting it at night — the robots may not mind, and the night shift becomes robotic while people work days. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Challenge the perception of industrial architecture as 'boxing']] | [[Visual impact and acceptance by neighbours]] |
| [[A two-to-three-tier building taxonomy]] | Fulfilment centres of ~100,000 m² (1,000,000 sq ft), potentially two or three levels internally; possibly a regional centre; then a local depot of ~5,000 m² or less on a city's edge or inside it — expensive land, cheap building, minimal fit-out, "in and out pretty much," not really storage. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Performing Despite the Headwinds – Understanding the drivers of European logistics performance]] | — |
| [[A vehicle maker rebrands as a mobility provider]] | Volvo positions itself not as a vehicle manufacturer but as a mobility provider with an obligation to sustainable development in the cities where it operates — including a heritage plaza around the building where the first Volvo was made over 100 years ago. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Plot to Place – Designing Thriving and Resilient Neighborhoods]] | — |
| [[A view raises memory by 10–25%]] | From a 2013 World Green Building Council survey: a good outside view (park, greenery, river) raised mental function and memory by 10 to 25%, and calls were handled faster. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-disputed">Disputed</span> | [[Criteria for a Green Building – Beyond energy performance LEED - BREEAM - WELL]] | [[Worker comfort and well-being]], [[Worker well-being]] |
| [[A warehouse heating a hospital]] | A Panattoni warehouse in Rotterdam carries far more PV than it needs and shares energy with local hospitals, with wind generation immediately behind the site. Energy sharing between buildings in a city is already legal and technically possible under Czech energy law. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Model to Reality – Sustainability in Industrial Buildings]] | [[Operational energy and on-site renewables]], [[Rooftop PV generation]] |
| [[A warehouse job needs six times the floor of an office job]] | The UK's standard employment density benchmarks give warehousing and distribution 70 m² GEA per full-time employee, rising to 80 m² for large-scale high-bay, against 36 m² for general industrial and 12 m² NIA for a general office. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-external">External</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Homes and Communities Agency, Employment Densities Guide, 2nd ed., 2010]] | [[Jobs created]] |
| [[A warehouse run by four people]] | A jd.com warehouse in China is reportedly staffed by four people, while an Ocado facility in the UK runs over 1,000 picking-and-sorting robots. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Performing Despite the Headwinds – Understanding the drivers of European logistics performance]] | [[Automation and operational efficiency of logistics]], [[Level of automation]] |
| [[A water treatment plant that is also a walk]] | A Danish water treatment plant is sunk and covered so the interior stays purely technical while the roof reads as continuous surrounding nature that people walk across. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | [[Public access and community use of logistics sites]] |
| [[A-class stock in CEE went from nothing to best-in-Europe in 30 years]] | 30 years ago there was no such thing as an A class modern industrial building in Central and Eastern Europe. It didn't exist. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Why CEE is the Hottest Location in Europe for supply chain, logistics and industrial Development]] | — |
| [[Absorbed airfields are a project type]] | Downsview Toronto — an old airfield becoming a district for 25,000 people, the biggest urban development in North America — is one instance of a recurring global type: Tegel, Bromma and others, where the city grew around and swallowed the airfield. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Plot to Place – Designing Thriving and Resilient Neighborhoods]] | — |
| [[Accelerants trade speed for strength]] | Extra accelerant can be dosed by a second screw in the nozzle to set material in 20–30 seconds, and you can add as much as you like — but layers that solidify immediately do not bond to the next layer, and the accelerant dose reduces final strength, so an ultra-high-performance mix can end up weaker than ordinary concrete. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Printing Against Gravity – From the construction site to the research lab]] | — |
| [[Access regulation is the strongest design lever]] | Urban vehicle access regulations — restricting which vehicle classes may enter which parts of the city and when — are quite strong policies shaping which last-mile vehicles are used, effectively forcing smaller electric vehicles and time-windowed delivery. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Integrated Urban Logistics – From last-mile to mobility hubs]] | [[Last-mile delivery emissions and traffic]] |
| [[Adaptability saves carbon but can't be counted]] | Adaptability and disassembly extend service life and avoid demolition, which is where the big savings are — yet the standards currently do not permit modelling that benefit, partly because a claimed 100-year lifespan is easy greenwashing. The real driver is not material durability but *moral* service life. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Logistics Development in the concept of certification and LCA]] | [[Adaptable and relocatable logistics structures]], [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Adaptability]] |
| [[Adaptation is a design-phase decision, not a facilities one]] | The assessment chain is exposure → probability of financial loss or extra investment → adaptation plan. The same adaptation measures can simply be taken into account inside the design phase for a new building instead of being bolted on later. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Beyond the Concrete – Designing Buildings That Last]] | — |
| [[Adaptation measures come with real cost ranges]] | The most useful output is a PDF summary pairing each risk with each adaptation measure, a range of investment needed, and sometimes a real-life built example with its associated cost — and because the tool is recent, those costs are close to what can actually be expected. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Beyond the Concrete – Designing Buildings That Last]] | — |
| [[Adaptive reuse gives a sense of place new construction cannot]] | Offered as a design principle from the brewery: the preserved malt house is what anchors the public experience. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Challenge the perception of industrial architecture as 'boxing']] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Public access and community use of logistics sites]] |
| [[Adiabatic humidification – cheap, hungry for space, no sterilisation]] | Spray humidification is far less energy-intensive than steam and cools the air as it works, but needs substantial plant space and cannot sterilise; steam is energy-hungry yet kills almost all bacteria in the air. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Criteria for a Green Building – Beyond energy performance LEED - BREEAM - WELL]] | [[Operational energy and on-site renewables]] |
| [[Adidas return rates close to 100%]] | Reported second-hand: at some Adidas logistics centres the return rate approaches 100%, driven by consumers ordering three sizes intending to keep one. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | — |
| [[Aesthetics is the missing agenda item]] | The industry does not discuss beauty, and integrating architecture, biodiversity and appearance is what makes this building type acceptable in an urban fabric. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Cloud in the city – data centres, urban development and implications]] | [[Visual impact and acceptance by neighbours]], [[Urban integration of logistics buildings]] |
| [[After BIM handover, nobody cares about the model]] | Once the ribbon is cut the design and BIM models are abandoned; the studio's next AI frontier is the production phase, where the accumulated model data is currently wasted. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Henning Larsen Playbook for Biobased Buildings]] | — |
| [[Against the exportable prototype]] | The industrial logic of inventing a product and scaling it globally is itself unsustainable: biogenic material should be harvested near where it is used. A prototype should carry a shared core plus components appropriated to local supply chains and end-of-life. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]] |
| [[AI adoption tripled in two years]] | European businesses using at least one AI technology rose from 8% in 2023 to roughly 20% in 2025, with a sharp North–South divide: about 42% of Danish businesses versus around 8% in Poland. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Geography of Growth – Cities, AI, and the future of property]] | — |
| [[AI can't help you innovate on straw]] | Prompting for a Henning Larsen building in straw and biogenic materials returned something close to their own existing Bestseller project — the model is biased toward existing data, which makes it structurally poor at innovation. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Henning Larsen Playbook for Biobased Buildings]] | — |
| [[AI governance as culture, not policy]] | Understand the bias, communicate openly (18 months ago staff turned their screens away when using Midjourney, treating it as cheating), let adoption be bottom-up, and accept full authorship — the Deloitte Australia lawsuit over bad citations was brought against Deloitte, not the tool. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Henning Larsen Playbook for Biobased Buildings]] | — |
| [[Always ask which scenario the tool assumes]] | A resilience tool built on an optimistic scenario will report that your building has few risks — and the optimistic scenarios are already being missed "by far." | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Beyond the Concrete – Designing Buildings That Last]] | — |
| [[Amazon 2014 vs 2022 – same floorplate, different building]] | Comparing Dobrovíz (2014) with Kojetín (2022) at similar floor area, 2024 operating data show the newer building far lower in energy use — attributed to envelope quality, heat pumps plus PV, ventilation efficiency, building shape, and a shift to largely robotic operation. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Model to Reality – Sustainability in Industrial Buildings]] | [[Operational energy and on-site renewables]], [[Energy use intensity]] |
| [[Amazon locates by unemployment rate]] | Amazon's buildings in the north of England sit there because the labour is there: a facility needing 3,000 people cannot be staffed in London. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Performing Despite the Headwinds – Understanding the drivers of European logistics performance]] | [[Jobs created]] |
| [[Amazon multi-storey – 60–70% self-supplied]] | The four-storey ~200,000 m² Amazon facility on a brownfield carries photovoltaics covering an estimated 60–70% of consumption and is described as fossil-free — the project that shifted the market's sense of what industrial can be. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Industrial Architecture as Living Infrastructure – Why future design is defined by systems, data, and adaptability, not just form]] | [[Operational energy and on-site renewables]], [[Land take of logistics buildings]], [[On-site renewable energy share]] |
| [[Ambition upgraded mid-project from do-no-harm to net-gain]] | The biodiversity target began as "don't affect biodiversity negatively" and was reformulated during the project, as the landscape strategies developed, into increasing site biodiversity. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | [[Biodiversity and ecological connectivity on logistics sites]], [[Biodiversity net gain]] |
| [[An honest I don't know on bioremediation]] | Asked about planting species to absorb truck oils on logistics sites, he declines to answer and redirects to Ramboll's landscape architects and to Henning Larsen. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Net Zero That Holds Up – Whole-Life Carbon, Design Choices, and Delivery]] | — |
| [[Architect as decision problem solver, not form maker]] | Against the image of the architect in a black t-shirt as form maker, they redefine the role for industrial work as a "decision problem solver" — doing calculations, studies and case studies specifically to reveal what the client actually needs. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Build Your Own Playground – Architecture where no one looks for it]] | — |
| [[Architect wrote the client's sustainability brief]] | The competition brief demanded high sustainability ambition without defining it. Henning Larsen researched Bestseller's own "Fashion Forward" strategy — written for fashion production, not buildings — and translated it into three building-scale ambitions that then governed every design decision. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | — |
| [[Architects design assets; money lives at portfolio level]] | The stated point of a Big4 team addressing architecture students is to force a zoom-out: connect the asset the designer is drawing to the corporate level, and connect both to risk management. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Beyond the Concrete – Designing Buildings That Last]] | — |
| [[Architecture as a human resource tool]] | A high-end office built inside an existing logistics warehouse, pitched to the client not as refurbishment but as a recruitment and retention instrument: salary alone no longer attracts talent to an out-of-town shed. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Build Your Own Playground – Architecture where no one looks for it]] | [[Worker comfort and well-being]], [[Worker well-being]] |
| [[Ask whether the warehouse works in a blackout]] | Resilience for logistics means asking operational questions certification does not: can trucks still reach the site in a flood, can the hall keep running through a blackout, what happens to a warehouse full of food. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Logistics Development in the concept of certification and LCA]] | — |
| [[Assessment is not optional at this scale]] | BREEAM and LEED criteria are designed in from the start via a separate specialist body; EIA covers much of the same ground; and because the money is usually a bank loan, the criteria are a financing condition. "In this size, the assessments are really necessary. It cannot happen without those." | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Challenge the perception of industrial architecture as 'boxing']] | — |
| [[Australia ships its iron ore around the world and buys it back as steel]] | Australia extracts iron ore in Western Australia, ships it to China, Japan or Korea for steelmaking, and ships the steel back — "insane" but rational, because no carbon price is embedded in the transaction. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Why CEE is the Hottest Location in Europe for supply chain, logistics and industrial Development]] | — |
| [[Automated trucks will not change the loading dock]] | Provided automated trucks keep the same dimensions, loading bays stay as they are — and the expectation is *more* bays, for more vehicle types. If the vehicle changes shape, the bay becomes a design question again. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Challenge the perception of industrial architecture as 'boxing']] | [[Automation and operational efficiency of logistics]], [[Number of docks]], [[Hynek Hrabík]] |
| [[Automation is a wage-driven substitution, not a technology story]] | Robotics, AS/RS and AGV picking are spreading specifically because labour costs are rising; if labour is 20–30% of supply-chain cost and automation removes most of it, the payback is large. The building consequence is height, and therefore a permitting fight. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Why CEE is the Hottest Location in Europe for supply chain, logistics and industrial Development]] | [[Automation and operational efficiency of logistics]], [[Level of automation]] |
| [[Basements are bad news, full stop]] | Putting anything in the ground is extremely carbon intensive. Even a multi-storey car park above ground beats parking below it. For an underground data centre proposed on biodiversity grounds, he says it wouldn't help biodiversity either — though an existing hole in the ground changes the maths. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Net Zero That Holds Up – Whole-Life Carbon, Design Choices, and Delivery]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Embodied carbon intensity]] |
| [[Bats reframed as a lighting problem]] | On the Sugar Valley (former Siemens factory, Munich) master plan, protected endangered bats drove dedicated high-level bat homes and, crucially, the outdoor lighting scheme — the office's lighting specialist calls himself an expert in "light and shadow design" so insects survive and bats aren't killed at the lamps. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | [[Biodiversity and ecological connectivity on logistics sites]] |
| [[Better toilets make politer shop assistants]] | The way into the Penny relationship was staff facilities, not the shop floor. The argument sold to the client was a chain of consequences: better back-of-house space produces less irritable shop assistants, which improves the customer experience. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Build Your Own Playground – Architecture where no one looks for it]] | [[Worker comfort and well-being]] |
| [[Beyond Czech water standards, into BREEAM's]] | Rainfall and infiltration for the whole site are calculated not only to Czech norms but to the stricter standards embedded in BREEAM certification, with additional modelling of alternative scenarios. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Model to Reality – Sustainability in Industrial Buildings]] | [[Stormwater and sealed surfaces on logistics sites]] |
| [[Big garages flatter your per-m² carbon]] | In the Skanska Dřevák study, the conventional buildings scored 22–23 kg CO2/m²/yr while their garages scored only 9; because the garages were large, the blended figure fell to 19. The timber building's higher headline number was only higher against that diluted total. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Logistics Development in the concept of certification and LCA]] | [[Embodied carbon intensity]] |
| [[Big systems mean big impact]] | Her closing argument for why designers should chase industrial and logistics commissions. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | — |
| [[Bigger objects print more easily than small ones]] | Counterintuitively, the worst thing to print is a small column: each layer takes so little time that material stacks up before it can gain strength. Prvok used a nozzle roughly 4 cm in diameter with a layer height of about 12 mm; reducing layer height to chase resolution can add around three extra hours of printing. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Printing Against Gravity – From the construction site to the research lab]] | — |
| [[Biggest cities are not the fastest]] | London, Paris and Madrid dominate on GDP scale but the fastest-growing European city economies sit in Central and Eastern Europe (Warsaw cited), because mature economies have less room to expand. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Geography of Growth – Cities, AI, and the future of property]] | — |
| [[BIM vs bill-of-quantities differed by only 5%]] | The same Skanska project was calculated both ways; the total GWP differed by only 5%, while the speed ratio was described as 3:2:1. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-disputed">Disputed</span> | [[Logistics Development in the concept of certification and LCA]] | [[Embodied carbon intensity]] |
| [[Biodiversity forbidden by food-hygiene law]] | On a food production site, strict limits on insects and rodents mean the client asks you *not* to introduce anything that improves biodiversity — because attracting life outside means poisoning everything outside to keep it out of the factory. International hygiene standards, not just Czech ones. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Challenge the perception of industrial architecture as 'boxing']] | [[Biodiversity and ecological connectivity on logistics sites]] |
| [[Biodiversity net gain, step by step]] | Ecologist-led biodiversity survey → baseline map of existing trees, species and protected species → coexistence and connectivity strategy against the build programme → biotope/species lists → recalculation against the baseline to quantify improvement. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | [[Biodiversity and ecological connectivity on logistics sites]], [[Biodiversity net gain]], [[Ecological connectivity]] |
| [[Biogenic carbon is a bonus, not a currency]] | A kilogram of dried timber holds ~0.5 kg carbon, equivalent to 1.8 kg biogenic CO2, showing as a negative on an EPD. Combining it with fossil emissions is "fundamentally wrong" because it masks inefficiency — he has seen projects 15 years ago deliberately use *more* timber to make the number more negative. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Net Zero That Holds Up – Whole-Life Carbon, Design Choices, and Delivery]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Embodied carbon intensity]] |
| [[Biogenic carbon nets to zero, not minus]] | Under the current standardised method, sequestered carbon is counted as negative at the start of the life cycle but must be returned to nature in the end-of-life modules — so a timber building does not finish in the negative, it finishes at roughly zero. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Logistics Development in the concept of certification and LCA]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Life-cycle impact by stage]] |
| [[Bioreceptivity belongs to clay, not cement]] | Research at the faculty explores how algae, moss and living organisms can grow on printed structures, but it is done in clay, described as a much more nature-friendly material for that purpose. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Printing Against Gravity – From the construction site to the research lab]] | [[Biodiversity and ecological connectivity on logistics sites]] |
| [[Biosphere is not an externality]] | She rejects the three-overlapping-circles model in favour of the "wedding cake" hierarchy: environment is not one competing interest but the context all life and all economic activity sits inside. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | — |
| [[Blackout blinds are technically possible and practically absurd]] | Adding internal blackout to a translucent envelope would mean doubling the system across "100 kilometres of line", doubling price and construction complexity — and in a clean industrial process textiles that collect dust are not allowed. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Challenge the perception of industrial architecture as 'boxing']] | [[Visual impact and acceptance by neighbours]] |
| [[BMW's pressure travels down two tiers]] | On the Hanon project in Kladno (a supplier of cooling devices to BMW), the end carmaker's electrification and quality demands forced all furnaces and process technology to electricity, PV across the roof including the old structure, heat pumps retrofitted into the existing building, and overpressure zones in finishing areas. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Model to Reality – Sustainability in Industrial Buildings]] | [[Operational energy and on-site renewables]] |
| [[BNG used outside Britain, as a design instrument]] | The team scored the site's baseline biodiversity (area, species count, plant age/trunk diameter) against the designed habitats using the British BNG standard. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | [[Biodiversity and ecological connectivity on logistics sites]], [[Biodiversity net gain]] |
| [[Boring numbers buy you the joy of life]] | The graphs-and-numbers front end is the price of admission: it is what later creates room for design. Their example of the unformed brief is a client arriving with "we have 2000 employees" and no idea how to divide them into teams. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Build Your Own Playground – Architecture where no one looks for it]] | — |
| [[Brand trust, not planning gain, is what opens the factory]] | Today the driver for opening industrial sites to the public is branding — the owner wants the public to be friends with the brand, and the shop front sells the whole brand, not just that factory's output. There is no legal obligation to open up; it is incentivised, not prescribed. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Challenge the perception of industrial architecture as 'boxing']] | [[Public access and community use of logistics sites]] |
| [[BREEAM LCA runs to 60 years]] | For the Greenpoint building in Prague, the BREEAM life-cycle assessment used a 60-year study period, flagged as notably long. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Criteria for a Green Building – Beyond energy performance LEED - BREEAM - WELL]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Whole-life carbon]] |
| [[BREEAM's warehouse typology is still generic underneath]] | BREEAM does have a dedicated warehouse typology, but it derives from the generic one, so it keeps asking about cycling, biodiversity and user comfort and cannot drop them.
