| [[Designers must stand in the field of conflict]] | She defines the designer's job as navigating conflicting requirements and owning the imperfect decision. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | — |
| [[Designers turned a blind eye to logistics]] | The profession's "blind belief in technical solutions" meant infrastructure, industry and logistics were never treated as a design challenge at all. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | — |
| [[Developer money into forest resilience]] | A net-zero pilot on the Škoda Transportation brownfield (a site that took ten years to unlock) is paired with private investment into Czech forests for resilience and CO2 capture — claimed as the first of its kind in CZ. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Industrial Architecture as Living Infrastructure – Why future design is defined by systems, data, and adaptability, not just form]] | — |
| [[Developers overbuilt, then vacancy caught them]] | The sector overshot: developers could not keep pace with pandemic demand, then "overstepped or they overbuilt," pushing vacancy up. He points to the lag between demand and delivery. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Performing Despite the Headwinds – Understanding the drivers of European logistics performance]] | — |
| [[Difficult ground can decide your carbon score]] | In that house, difficult ground conditions forced foundations about 1.5 m deep; the concrete became the single biggest hotspot in the whole LCA, outweighing every above-ground material choice. It raises the question of whether uniform carbon limits are usable for every site. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Logistics Development in the concept of certification and LCA]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Embodied carbon intensity]] |
| [[Digging materials up costs more than buying new]] | The economics of reclaimed material fail because extraction from an existing building can be more expensive than new supply — which is exactly why you design for disassembly now. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Challenge the perception of industrial architecture as 'boxing']] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Design for disassembly score]] |
| [[Digital models are not yet market standard]] | HVAC designers still size systems from codes and standards alone, with the simplifications that implies; detailed energy modelling has to be brought in deliberately, project by project. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Model to Reality – Sustainability in Industrial Buildings]] | — |
| [[Distant material extraction damages distant biotopes]] | Sourcing from far away harms the biotopes at the extraction site and adds transport CO2, which is an argument for site-derived materials — though she notes the Bestseller site is "probably just sand," ruling out rammed earth there. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Biodiversity stress from materials]] |
| [[Don't dig down vs biodiversity]] | Confronted with the previous lecturer's carbon rule against excavation, she concedes it is right on carbon alone, then argues the counter-case: sinking a structure yields a large permeable soil surface that handles rainwater. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | [[Stormwater and sealed surfaces on logistics sites]], [[Impervious surface ratio]] |
| [[Don't force the wrong second life on a building]] | For data centres he would not compromise operational efficiency to reuse a shell: a 10%-less-efficient data centre likely loses over its life. His preferred move is relocation, not demolition — put the data centre elsewhere and give the existing building a use it suits. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Net Zero That Holds Up – Whole-Life Carbon, Design Choices, and Delivery]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Whole-life carbon]], [[Julia Godard Lombard]], [[Katrin Rybinskiy]] |
| [[Don't get obsessed with the 10%]] | She pushes back on her own metric: the essential shift is telling a story about nature from the outset; the number is a support, not the point. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | [[Biodiversity net gain]] |
| [[Don't let every unit be 2,000 m²]] | Mix rental unit sizes and price points deliberately, so a 50 m² yoga studio sits alongside large tenancies — this is what varies opening hours and offerings across the day. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Plot to Place – Designing Thriving and Resilient Neighborhoods]] | [[Urban integration of logistics buildings]], [[Mixed-use ratio]], [[Marek Nový]] |
| [[Dublin planned for data centre demand instead of reacting to it]] | Dublin integrated data centre demand into its forward energy and land-use planning alongside hospitals, schools, housing and offices, deliberately steering the facilities into a chosen part of the city — the opposite of developers buying whatever plot they could find. He calls urban planning a fundamental and badly underused tool in Europe. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Cloud in the city – data centres, urban development and implications]] | [[Urban integration of logistics buildings]] |
| [[E-commerce is not the main occupier — and is shrinking]] | Pure-play e-commerce fell from about 17% of European take-up in 2021 to about 8% in 2025. Online penetration peaked at roughly 15–25% of retail sales (up to ~35% in the UK during the pandemic) and has since fallen back and levelled off. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-disputed">Disputed</span> | [[Performing Despite the Headwinds – Understanding the drivers of European logistics performance]] | — |
| [[Each IT generation makes the building hotter]] | Every new generation of chips is more powerful and therefore produces more heat, so the cooling load keeps escalating even as the buildings grow — which explains why the efficiency curve flattened. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Cloud in the city – data centres, urban development and implications]] | [[Waste heat and cooling of data centres]], [[Power usage effectiveness]] |
| [[Easier to drill wood than reinforced concrete]] | Adaptability argued at the most practical level: a timber building tolerates late changes in a way reinforced concrete does not. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Build Your Own Playground – Architecture where no one looks for it]] | [[Adaptable and relocatable logistics structures]] |
| [[Efficiency has hit a plateau at 1.5]] | PUE dropped sharply about 15 years ago (~2.5 in 2007) but has stalled around 1.5 for the last six or seven years, against a theoretical ideal of 1. Individual projects can go below 1.5; the industry collectively is struggling to improve. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Cloud in the city – data centres, urban development and implications]] | [[Waste heat and cooling of data centres]], [[Power usage effectiveness]] |
| [[Eight degrees quintuples walking distance]] | On Singapore's North-South Corridor — an eight-lane traffic corridor becoming ~25 km of new urban development — a deliberately low-tech urban cooling toolbox targets an 8°C drop in perceived temperature; that drop multiplies comfortable walking distance by five. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Plot to Place – Designing Thriving and Resilient Neighborhoods]] | [[Outdoor microclimate around logistics buildings]], [[Thermal comfort]], [[Tree count and canopy cover]] |
| [[Electric trucks as a noise-permitting instrument]] | Raised as an open design question: as electric trucks replace diesel, the change in noise levels could alter what is permissible near cities. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Model to Reality – Sustainability in Industrial Buildings]] | [[Visual impact and acceptance by neighbours]], [[Noise level]] |
| [[Electric trucks don't buy you a green corridor]] | Asked whether electrifying fleets enables green corridors, she says it probably makes no difference: the corridor problem is spatial, not fuel-based. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | — |
| [[Electrification is a system shift, not a vehicle shift]] | Switching fleets to electric requires charging stations, battery swapping and grid capacity designed into streets, logistics hubs and buildings, forcing coordination between transport planning, energy planning and urban development. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Integrated Urban Logistics – From last-mile to mobility hubs]] | [[Last-mile delivery emissions and traffic]] |
| [[Electrification may shrink automotive storage demand]] | German automotive manufacturers have historically stored large volumes of combustion-engine parts; a simpler electric drivetrain implies less parts storage. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Performing Despite the Headwinds – Understanding the drivers of European logistics performance]] | — |
| [[Electrifying the car is the lazy answer]] | Manufacturers respond to one person in a fossil car by making the hardware better; the flexibility strategy is putting five people in it. Better hardware versus better utilisation. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Plot to Place – Designing Thriving and Resilient Neighborhoods]] | [[Space utilisation]] |
| [[Emptying bins that aren't full]] | The ROSE project replaced fixed collection schedules with sensor-based fill-level clustering, vehicle assignment and dynamic routing, piloted in Montana (Bulgaria) and Cascais (Portugal) — showing even highly standardised public services carry large hidden inefficiency. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Integrated Urban Logistics – From last-mile to mobility hubs]] | — |
| [[Enabling development as an inherited burden]] | Municipalities routinely require the incoming developer to build infrastructure sized for other, unrelated parts of the city, on top of remediation costs. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Industrial Architecture as Living Infrastructure – Why future design is defined by systems, data, and adaptability, not just form]] | — |
| [[Energy chapter is half of a LEED Gold]] | LEED Gold requires at least 60 credits; the energy and atmosphere chapter carries 33 credits, roughly half of a Gold on its own — and it is the chapter that forces detailed dynamic simulation, making it time-consuming for the consultants. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Criteria for a Green Building – Beyond energy performance LEED - BREEAM - WELL]] | [[Operational energy and on-site renewables]] |
| [[Energy is 1% of a company's budget]] | For a typical office, retail or shopping-centre operator, energy costs are around 1% of the overall company budget, which is why owners are not strongly motivated by energy-efficiency measures; the people-related metrics move the budget far more. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Criteria for a Green Building – Beyond energy performance LEED - BREEAM - WELL]] | [[Operational energy and on-site renewables]] |
| [[Energy performance is a financing instrument]] | Every project carries an EPC that must be shown to banks and investors; landing below a threshold value buys better financing terms. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Model to Reality – Sustainability in Industrial Buildings]] | [[Energy use intensity]] |
| [[England's best farmland is going a hundred times faster]] | CPRE research found nearly 14,500 hectares of England's Best and Most Versatile agricultural land lost to development since 2010, with the annual rate rising from 60 hectares in 2010 to over 6,000 hectares by 2022 — enough land to grow at least 250,000 tonnes of vegetables a year, while 26,000 hectares of brownfield sat available. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-external">External</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[CPRE, 2022]] | [[Land take of logistics buildings]] |
| [[ESG entered the risk register only ~2 years ago]] | ESG is described as the pillar "gaining more traction in the market in the last, I would say 2 years," slotting alongside financial, market, legal, IT/cyber and HR risk — with the environmental leg mapping directly onto buildings. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Beyond the Concrete – Designing Buildings That Last]] | — |
| [[ESG survived the politics, via the banks]] | Despite political retreat from environmental commitments, the rules and financial plumbing still embed it: banks and regulators build climate and ESG factors into lending decisions, which determines project funding and where money flows into cities. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Geography of Growth – Cities, AI, and the future of property]] | — |
| [[EU taxonomy – not mandatory, but at the end it is]] | Formally voluntary, yet without it financing is effectively unobtainable. In practice it means blower-door testing and thermal imaging for envelope integrity, full LCA covering embodied plus operational carbon, and a high-grade BMS — and these KPIs now sit inside the general contractor's contract. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Model to Reality – Sustainability in Industrial Buildings]] | [[Whole-life carbon]] |
| [[Europe finally has a soil law]] | Directive (EU) 2025/2360, the first EU-wide Soil Monitoring Law, was agreed in April 2025 on the finding that over 60% of EU soils are unhealthy. It obliges member states to monitor soil health on a common methodology and to apply mitigation principles for soil sealing and soil removal in spatial planning — but the 2050 healthy-soils goal remains non-binding. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-external">External</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Council of the EU, 2025]] | [[Land take of logistics buildings]] |
| [[Europe is behind the US and China on deployment]] | She distinguishes Europe's level of testing and piloting from actual deployment and concedes a big gap versus North America and China on autonomous urban delivery, attributing it to fragmented national and regional regulation, the geography of many adjacent countries, and differences in governmental support and funding. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Integrated Urban Logistics – From last-mile to mobility hubs]] | — |
| [[Europe seals 1.5 km² of land every day]] | Net land take in EU cities and their commuting zones ran at 540 km²/year in 2018–2021, up 32% on the 410 km²/year of 2012–2018. Most of it comes off arable land (268 km²/year) and pasture (182 km²/year). To hit "no net land take by 2050" Europe would have had to cut this by roughly 27 km² every year from 2022 — it is going the other way. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-external">External</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[European Environment Agency, Net land take in cities and commuting zones, 2024]] | [[Land take of logistics buildings]], [[Land take]] |
| [[Europe's urban share is halving by 2050]] | Europe's major cities produced just under a third of global city GDP in 2000, just above 20% by 2025, and modelling puts it near 15% by 2050 — Europe still growing, but far slower than China, India and Asia-Pacific. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Geography of Growth – Cities, AI, and the future of property]] | — |
| [[Even the investors are asking whether AI use will change]] | At a London conference days earlier, very large investors were questioning whether the current pattern of AI use — and therefore the infrastructure it demands — will persist; the industry has no answer. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Cloud in the city – data centres, urban development and implications]] | — |
| [[Every surface recruited – wetland, roof meadow, green facade]] | The landscape is staged as a sequence — arrival through forested landscape, open wetland that also handles rainwater, a large roof meadow for workers' lunch breaks — with roof and facade both counted as biodiversity surface. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | [[Biodiversity and ecological connectivity on logistics sites]], [[Function stacking index]], [[Green roof coverage]] |
| [[Every version makes yesterday's exemplar mediocre]] | ČSOB SHQ was certified under LEED v3; v4/4.1 are current and v5 was expected mid-year. Newer versions are stricter on most credits, so the same building assessed today would score substantially fewer credits despite still being top property in the country. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Criteria for a Green Building – Beyond energy performance LEED - BREEAM - WELL]] | — |
| [[Everyone is a carbon assessor]] | Assessment splits into designer-level (fast, rough, decision-driving), discipline-level, and professional/codified — and speed matters as much as rigour. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Net Zero That Holds Up – Whole-Life Carbon, Design Choices, and Delivery]] | — |
| [[Exit-grade quality is enforced by the fund, not the code]] | Because each asset must survive comparison with the whole European market and pass multiple due diligences on sale, the investor exit drives build quality upward. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Industrial Architecture as Living Infrastructure – Why future design is defined by systems, data, and adaptability, not just form]] | — |
| [[Expandable units are wanted but planning kills them]] | Expanding means either a two-to-three-year build-and-permit process or waiting for a neighbour to vacate. The workaround the market uses is outsourcing to 3PLs during growth. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Performing Despite the Headwinds – Understanding the drivers of European logistics performance]] | [[Adaptable and relocatable logistics structures]], [[Adaptability]], [[Amy Naprstek]] |
| [[Exposure is easy; vulnerability is the design question]] | Exposure is "really easy to calculate" from location and hazard data, but vulnerability depends entirely on what adaptation measures are already built into the design or in operation. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Beyond the Concrete – Designing Buildings That Last]] | — |
| [[Facility managers inherit a building blind]] | A frequent failure: the building is built, property management arrives, but never receives sufficient as-built documentation, and the information about settings, regulation and measurement systems is simply lost. Handing that data over is itself part of commissioning. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Criteria for a Green Building – Beyond energy performance LEED - BREEAM - WELL]] | — |
| [[Fælledby outcome numbers]] | Computational optimisation is credited with delivering, for ~5,000 new residents: 25% with direct access to nature, 30% with a view to the park, 100% with a view to green areas, and a balcony for everyone. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Henning Larsen Playbook for Biobased Buildings]] | — |
| [[False precision is nonsense]] | A figure quoted to three decimal places is meaningless. Uncertainty is inevitable; what matters is being transparent about its sources (project maturity, quantity data, carbon data). | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Net Zero That Holds Up – Whole-Life Carbon, Design Choices, and Delivery]] | [[Embodied carbon intensity]] |
| [[Farmland bans are the engine of brownfield revival]] | European countries are increasingly restricting warehousing on fertile arable land, in some places outright, pushing developers toward brownfields. Old steelworks and contaminated production sites in CEE are being demolished and converted to small business units serving inner-city demand. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Why CEE is the Hottest Location in Europe for supply chain, logistics and industrial Development]] | [[Land take of logistics buildings]] |
| [[Fastest-growing segment in real estate]] | Roughly 11,000–12,000 data centres exist globally, consuming about 1.5–2% of the world's electricity and accounting for somewhere in the region of 1–3% of global CO2; the market is estimated to grow 16% per annum to 2030. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-disputed">Disputed</span> | [[Cloud in the city – data centres, urban development and implications]] | — |
| [[Fewer than 5% of UK industrial roofs have panels]] | Under 5% of UK industrial and commercial rooftops carry PV. The blockers are structural (~20 kg per panel on lightweight decks), contractual (10-year industrial leases against multi-decade solar assets, with tenants responsible for the roof), and infrastructural — UK government reporting cites grid connection waits of up to 15 years, with the queue growing tenfold in five years. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-external">External</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[EQT, Why it's so difficult to put solar panels on warehouse roofs]] | [[Operational energy and on-site renewables]], [[Rooftop PV generation]], [[Structural utilisation]] |
| [[Fire isn't timber's real problem — acoustics is]] | In their mass timber practice, moisture is manageable and fire is not the biggest issue; acoustics and vibration are. Concrete toppings are added for thermal mass and acoustic/vibration control, not for structure. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Henning Larsen Playbook for Biobased Buildings]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]] |
| [[First CEE timber warehouse permit — and unsellable]] | Panattoni were first to obtain a permit for a 40,000 m² warehouse with a timber roof structure in Czechia/CEE. The candid admission: the price is currently so high that building it and selling it to a client is almost impossible. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Model to Reality – Sustainability in Industrial Buildings]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Construction cost]] |
| [[Fit-out can cost more than the building]] | In technically advanced warehouses the internal fit-out (automation, racking, robotics) often costs at least as much as the shell, and frequently significantly more. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Performing Despite the Headwinds – Understanding the drivers of European logistics performance]] | [[Automation and operational efficiency of logistics]], [[Construction cost]] |
| [[Five years of continuous supply-chain shock]] | COVID, nearshoring, inflation, the current oil crisis and the Suez blockage have hit in succession; each pushes companies toward relocating production and storage closer to consumers, and toward decentralisation. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Why CEE is the Hottest Location in Europe for supply chain, logistics and industrial Development]] | — |
| [[Flexibility is time, not just hardware]] | Designing changeable systems is only the "hardware side"; the robust strategy is spatial quality good enough to host a school, apartments or offices in succession, plus temporal flexibility — public access to buildings standing empty most of the day. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Plot to Place – Designing Thriving and Resilient Neighborhoods]] | [[Adaptable and relocatable logistics structures]], [[Public access and community use of logistics sites]], [[Adaptability]], [[Elsa Hauksdottir]] |
| [[Floor-area definitions move your number 5–20%]] | Dozens of incompatible floor-area definitions worldwide (inner face, outer face, midpoint of wall; corridors in or out). The choice alone shifts a kgCO2/m² figure by 5 to 20%, before any design change. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Net Zero That Holds Up – Whole-Life Carbon, Design Choices, and Delivery]] | [[Embodied carbon intensity]] |
| [[For Czechia the risks are drought, floods, precipitation]] | Citing the Ministry of Environment: the biggest climate risks for the Czech Republic are drought, floods and precipitation. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Beyond the Concrete – Designing Buildings That Last]] | [[Stormwater and sealed surfaces on logistics sites]] |
| [[Forecast – 9% unlevered, 13–15% geared]] | Prime European logistics returns of ~9% per year unlevered over five years, rising to 13–15% with gearing, on vacancy falling to ~4% and rental growth of ~2–2.5% a year — well below the 2021–25 run of up to 10% a year in the UK and 9.5% in Czechia. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Performing Despite the Headwinds – Understanding the drivers of European logistics performance]] | — |
| [[Forklifts, not robots, forced the concrete]] | Phase-one capacity leaves part of the terminal unautomated, running manual racking and forklifts; point loads of ~6 tonnes through the wheels made timber structurally unreasonable there. Elsewhere the timber slab still needs a concrete topping; offices keep bare timber. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Structural utilisation]] |
| [[Foundation ownership as the reason for the openness]] | Henning Larsen sits inside the 18,000-person Ramboll Group, which is foundation-owned: profits flow back to humanitarian and research grants, which is what funds the Open Details platform and the research programme. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Henning Larsen Playbook for Biobased Buildings]] | — |
| [[Four coping strategies, including doing nothing]] | Risk responses: accept and do nothing (a legitimate strategy in some cases), transfer to another party, mitigate, or insure. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Beyond the Concrete – Designing Buildings That Last]] | — |
| [[Four habitat types as design vocabulary]] | Wetland, forest, moorland, green façade — each deployed where it did double duty: wetland beside the truck yard, forest wrapping the car park, moorland meadow across the terminal roof, green façade on the high bays. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | [[Biodiversity and ecological connectivity on logistics sites]], [[Function stacking index]] |
| [[Four pillars, with location as the fourth]] | SBTool CZ scores environmental, social and economic pillars plus a fourth — location: public transport connection, what is in the surroundings, whether a car is needed. Named as the distinguishing feature versus LEED and BREEAM. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Logistics Development in the concept of certification and LCA]] | [[Public transport accessibility]] |
| [[France already legislates when your yard lights go off]] | The arrêté of 27 December 2018 requires interior and facade lighting of non-residential premises to be switched off one hour after occupancy ends or by 1am, whichever is earlier, and not relit before 7am; uncovered car parks must go dark two hours after activity ends. It caps correlated colour temperature at 3000 K (2400 K in nature reserves) and upward light ratio below 1%. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-external">External</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Ministère de la Transition écologique, arrêté du 27 décembre 2018]] | [[Visual impact and acceptance by neighbours]], [[Biodiversity and ecological connectivity on logistics sites]] |
| [[France maps its ecology before it maps its plots]] | The Trame verte et bleue, established by the Grenelle 1 (2009) and Grenelle 2 (2010) laws, obliges regions to identify ecological continuities — reservoirs and corridors, terrestrial and aquatic — in their SRADDET schemas, which then cascade into municipal PLU/PLUi plans; the 2021 climate law made ecological-continuity assessment mandatory in local planning documents. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-external">External</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Ministère de la Transition écologique, Trame verte et bleue]] | [[Biodiversity and ecological connectivity on logistics sites]], [[Ecological connectivity]] |
| [[Free night cooling is still the cheapest move]] | Before any mechanical system, the stack effect remains the first and cheapest ventilation strategy: open high and low, purge the building overnight, then hold the cooled air. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Criteria for a Green Building – Beyond energy performance LEED - BREEAM - WELL]] | [[Operational energy and on-site renewables]], [[Natural ventilation potential]] |
| [[Free tool, 15–30 minutes, no registration for the basics]] | R4RE ("resilience for real estate"), a French platform they discovered "like a month or two ago", is free for the basic exposure/vulnerability diagnosis and measure list, needs no registration at that level, and takes 15–20 (max 30) minutes. Simulations and adaptation planning sit behind a paywall — with the aside that asking the sales team for a demo yields roughly 30 days of full access. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Beyond the Concrete – Designing Buildings That Last]] | — |
| [[From ~12 to 6.6 kg CO2e per m² per yr]] | The figure fell through roughly 8.9 at tender to about 6.6 once generic EPDs were replaced with specific ones. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Embodied carbon intensity]] |
| [[From 1% public to 30% public, plus 20% more greenery]] | The brewery project measured its own impact as a KPI: the site went from 1% public to 30% public area, with around 20% more greenery added. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Challenge the perception of industrial architecture as 'boxing']] | [[Public access and community use of logistics sites]], [[Publicly accessible area]], [[Green area ratio]] |
| [[From 20,000 households to a country]] | An average conventional data centre equals roughly 20,000 households; a hyperscale one about 100,000; the largest under construction as of late last year the equivalent of 2 million households; the largest announced, 5 million households. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Cloud in the city – data centres, urban development and implications]] | — |
| [[From 22 certified projects to over a thousand]] | In 2012 Czechia had only 22 certified projects; by early this year there were more than 1,000 LEED, BREEAM and WELL certified buildings — against one or two DGNB buildings in the whole country. The 2008 ČSOB North HQ is named as the break point. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Criteria for a Green Building – Beyond energy performance LEED - BREEAM - WELL]] | — |
| [[From near-bankrupt to global benchmark in one generation]] | In the 1980s Copenhagen had 18% unemployment, heavy pollution and near bankruptcy. The point is not that everyone should copy Copenhagen but that systemic urban change is demonstrably possible within a generation. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Plot to Place – Designing Thriving and Resilient Neighborhoods]] | — |
| [[From static curb allocation to dynamic curb management]] | Curb use should shift from fixed designation to time-based reallocation (delivery in the morning peak, then parking or public space afterwards), supported by digital booking, pricing and enforcement; curb inefficiency degrades the whole transport system. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Integrated Urban Logistics – From last-mile to mobility hubs]] | [[Last-mile delivery emissions and traffic]] |
| [[Full automation would need power plants bigger than the logistics]] | Pushing warehouses to drone-and-robot-only operation is self-defeating in energy terms: the computing infrastructure required would demand power plants and data centres larger than the logistics facilities they serve. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Build Your Own Playground – Architecture where no one looks for it]] | [[Automation and operational efficiency of logistics]], [[Level of automation]] |
| [[Fully built, barely used]] | That same fully electrified plant is currently running at roughly 20–30% of production with all technology already installed and waiting. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Model to Reality – Sustainability in Industrial Buildings]] | — |
| [[Gas kept only as an emergency line]] | On the newest projects: ZF in north Czechia fully electric with heat pumps and large PV and no gas at all; on another project gas exists solely as emergency backup — plus carbon compensation through credits arranged with a local forest owner. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Model to Reality – Sustainability in Industrial Buildings]] | [[Operational energy and on-site renewables]] |
| [[Generic data early, EPDs late]] | Generic data (aggregated, e.g. a 90th percentile of specific datasets) belongs at concept stage when the actual products are unknown; EPD-derived specific data belongs once products are chosen. Differences between producers are large and trace back to their technologies and fuels. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Logistics Development in the concept of certification and LCA]] | [[Embodied carbon intensity]] |
| [[Generic EPDs first, product-specific later, contractually locked]] | LCA ran through every design stage using the 2015-generation Danish method alongside BREEAM. Early stages used generic/average EPD data; high-impact materials were swapped to product-specific EPDs, and those products became a binding requirement in the contractor's tender. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Embodied carbon intensity]] |
| [[Geopolitics assigns tenants to countries]] | Chinese EV manufacturers and suppliers are clustering rapidly in Hungary and Slovakia — the "friendly countries" — while he receives almost no Chinese enquiries for Czechia, Poland or Germany. Separately, Western investors routinely instruct him to present only Czech locations and explicitly refuse Slovakia and Hungary. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Why CEE is the Hottest Location in Europe for supply chain, logistics and industrial Development]] | — |
| [[Germany lost the high value, not just the low]] | Beyond low- and mid-value production moving to China and India, Germany has lost ground in higher-value advanced manufacturing — automotive, machinery, electronics and engineering supply chains. Munich, Stuttgart and northern Italy show declining output. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Geography of Growth – Cities, AI, and the future of property]] | — |
| [[Germany's answer – trade tax plus designated land]] | Sarah Friedrich explains the German counter-model: municipalities earn trade tax from logistics assets they host, and planning designates areas that must be made available for industry. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Model to Reality – Sustainability in Industrial Buildings]] | — |
| [[Give nature a lawyer]] | She points to New Zealand and indigenous legal traditions where nature is granted standing and representation, so it can be a party to a lawsuit. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | — |
| [[Give the unloved leftover land to the data centre]] | A Ramboll architects' proposal puts a data centre in the residual space between motorways: noisy, poorly accessed, effectively undevelopable for anything else — and therefore, he suggests, a place where this programme could go. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Cloud in the city – data centres, urban development and implications]] | [[Urban integration of logistics buildings]] |
| [[Glare is judged at the single worst seat]] | Spatial daylight glare and daylight glare probability are both evaluated annually and only for occupied hours, and the analysis is run at one critical point deliberately chosen as the worst case — typically on the south facade. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Criteria for a Green Building – Beyond energy performance LEED - BREEAM - WELL]] | [[Worker comfort and well-being]] |
| [[Glulam on every unit, a first for Irish logistics parks]] | Nexus is the first logistics park in Ireland to offer glulam as an option on every unit. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-external">External</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[IPUT, Nexus Logistics Park website]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Embodied carbon intensity]] |
| [[Glulam spans like steel, as the developer puts it]] | Glue laminated timber delivers the same strength and spans as steel. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-external">External</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[IPUT, Nexus Logistics Park website]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Embodied carbon intensity]] |
| [[Going above the traffic, not through it]] | The USafe project answered emergency congestion delays not with faster ground vehicles but hybrid electric/hydrogen drones plus air traffic management, piloted in Zaragoza and Tartu — reframing logistics innovation as urban resilience and adding an aerial layer to the urban logistics system. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Integrated Urban Logistics – From last-mile to mobility hubs]] | [[Last-mile delivery emissions and traffic]], [[Johannes Berger]] |
| [[Green roofs are a biodiversity investment, not a carbon one]] | They don't help with carbon, and on a single-storey large-span logistics roof the added mass would massively increase structure. They make sense where column centres are narrow or the building is already multi-storey. The reframing: if you double the structure to carry the roof, what habitat was destroyed to win that material? | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Net Zero That Holds Up – Whole-Life Carbon, Design Choices, and Delivery]] | [[Biodiversity and ecological connectivity on logistics sites]], [[Green roof coverage]], [[Structural utilisation]], [[Biodiversity stress from materials]], [[Ece Yasar]] |
| [[Green roofs are not a substitute for ground]] | A systematic review of 77 peer-reviewed studies found species richness consistently higher at ground-level sites than on roofs for fungi, birds, bees and other arthropods, and concluded plainly that "green roofs should not be used as a substitute for other green spaces." Substrate depth was the dominant design variable — deep substrates raised plant and arthropod richness across 11 studies, with at least 15 cm needed for overwintering insects — while greater building height reduced spider and vascular plant richness. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-external">External</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Knapp, Schmauck & Zehnsdorf, Sustainability 11(20) – 5846, 2019]] | [[Biodiversity and ecological connectivity on logistics sites]], [[Green roof coverage]], [[Native species richness]] |
| [[Green roofs vs LCA — an admitted conflict]] | Putting nature on a roof adds substantial structural load, which worsens the carbon calculation; she names this as a genuine conflict. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Green roof coverage]], [[Structural utilisation]] |
| [[Greenfield today, city tomorrow]] | Even a greenfield site may become part of a future city; a building that does not contribute to urbanism now "might already be set up to failure in the future." | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Challenge the perception of industrial architecture as 'boxing']] | [[Urban integration of logistics buildings]] |
| [[Grey space – buildings sized for Black Friday]] | Occupiers deliberately hold slack — running at maybe 50–70% utilisation for most of the year so they can hit 100% from September through Christmas. Buildings are therefore dimensioned for an annual peak, and this slack is nearly impossible for outside advisors to measure. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Performing Despite the Headwinds – Understanding the drivers of European logistics performance]] | [[Adaptable and relocatable logistics structures]], [[Space utilisation]], [[Elsa Hauksdottir]] |
| [[Grid capacity becomes a planning constraint]] | For planners the question is not just siting but whether local energy systems can support data centres sustainably — including whether they squeeze residential and commercial supply in the same city. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Geography of Growth – Cities, AI, and the future of property]] | — |
| [[Grid decarbonisation is the one win happening without us]] | The last 10–20 years of grid decarbonisation and renewables rollout is the largest impact achieved, and is effectively happening in the background. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Net Zero That Holds Up – Whole-Life Carbon, Design Choices, and Delivery]] | — |
| [[GWP is heading into the statutory energy certificate]] | He expects global warming potential to become part of the next version of the legally mandated energy performance certificate, meaning embodied carbon would be calculated as part of the ordinary building permit process rather than voluntarily. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Criteria for a Green Building – Beyond energy performance LEED - BREEAM - WELL]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Embodied carbon intensity]] |
| [[Habitats copied from the surrounding landscape, not invented]] | The introduced biotopes deliberately mirror surrounding landscape types rather than importing exotic planting — "not about introducing a tropical garden" — so the design reinforces the nature already present. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | [[Biodiversity and ecological connectivity on logistics sites]], [[Native species richness]] |

| [[Half of loading zones misused]] | Around 50% of loading zones are used incorrectly, and cities still rely on manual, reactive patrol-based enforcement; the Smart Zone Enforcement pilot in Bucharest and Istanbul combined sensors, digital booking and an officer app to alert on violations instead of random patrolling. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Integrated Urban Logistics – From last-mile to mobility hubs]] | [[Last-mile delivery emissions and traffic]] |
