| [[He opens by disclaiming the discipline]] | I'm an economist, I'm not an architect, so I'm not smart enough to do this degree. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Geography of Growth – Cities, AI, and the future of property]] | — |
| [[Height buys efficiency and buys a fire problem]] | Going taller improves automation efficiency but triggers in-rack sprinklers, ventilation and exhaust systems. Fire suppression is "super expensive", so there is a trade-off. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Why CEE is the Hottest Location in Europe for supply chain, logistics and industrial Development]] | [[Automation and operational efficiency of logistics]], [[Construction cost]] |
| [[Hidden trade-offs are built into scoring]] | Chasing points can produce nonsense on a building: a measure that gains points in one criterion can do harm elsewhere. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Logistics Development in the concept of certification and LCA]] | — |
| [[High energy costs are deindustrialising Europe]] | European energy prices were already above the global average before the Russia–Ukraine war; sustained high costs raise production costs, cut competitiveness and push new capacity to lower-cost regions, hitting energy-intensive automotive and chemicals hardest. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Geography of Growth – Cities, AI, and the future of property]] | — |
| [[Hiring shifts from drawing to narrative]] | AI changed what the studio recruits for: less pure architectural production, more the ability to build a narrative, find the right data and story, and spend time on site with people. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Henning Larsen Playbook for Biobased Buildings]] | — |
| [[Honest gap – valuing nature awaits better tools]] | Asked how to calculate carbon and runoff performance of a designed ecosystem, she declines to claim expertise and says she hopes emerging AI-based programs will help. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | — |
| [[House prices +90%, incomes +10%]] | Indexed to 2015, European house prices are up nearly 90% (and expected to hold ~90% above 2015 levels by 2030) while household disposable income rose only around 10%; rents show a similar divergence. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Geography of Growth – Cities, AI, and the future of property]] | — |
| [[Houses lose kilowatts, warehouses lose megawatts]] | Large volumes, extreme ceiling heights and steep vertical temperature gradients make ventilation, cooling and heat-pump design "a completely different game" from housing. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Model to Reality – Sustainability in Industrial Buildings]] | [[Operational energy and on-site renewables]], [[Heating energy demand]] |
| [[How Much Does Your Building Weigh, Mr. Foster]] | He recommends the documentary How Much Does Your Building Weigh, Mr. Foster as the question of building resourcefully: the more a building weighs, the more trucks it takes to build it. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | — |
| [[Hub placement decided without data]] | Cities typically lack visibility into logistics flows and make hub siting decisions without appropriate data; the LogiHub project built a planning platform fusing mobility data with spatial data (loading zones, warehouses, infrastructure) to identify optimal hub locations — a design tool, not a building. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Integrated Urban Logistics – From last-mile to mobility hubs]] | [[Last-mile delivery emissions and traffic]], [[Delivery catchment]], [[Roshni Basu]] |
| [[Hub siting is still trial and error]] | Where to place a consolidation hub is an unresolved trade-off between operator cost efficiency and the availability of good space, and cities are learning by doing. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Integrated Urban Logistics – From last-mile to mobility hubs]] | [[Last-mile delivery emissions and traffic]] |
| [[Humans off the top of the triangle]] | People should no longer sit at the apex of the hierarchy but inside a circular system, with the mobility system and the nature system driving the plan; a hybrid of the European perimeter block and the village typology produced a dense U-shaped block type. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Plot to Place – Designing Thriving and Resilient Neighborhoods]] | — |
| [[Humidity, not Celsius, is the barrier]] | The walkability problem in tropical growing cities is not temperature alone but humidity — and growing cities worldwide are now generating their own accelerated local climate. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Plot to Place – Designing Thriving and Resilient Neighborhoods]] | [[Outdoor microclimate around logistics buildings]], [[Thermal comfort]] |
| [[Hybrid work has broken the desk count]] | Attendance peaks Tuesday–Thursday at around 80% and collapses to about 20% on Monday and lower on Friday: 800 employees no longer means 800 desks, and the recovered area goes to green space, fitness and amenity. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Build Your Own Playground – Architecture where no one looks for it]] | — |
| [[Hyperlocal and citywide, both]] | Neighbourhoods need functions for residents and destinations that pull in the whole city; other recommendations include slow-pace mobility with very small plazas, aligning placemaking with municipal planners for a mutual business case, defining KPIs for activities, and innovative ownership models for ground-floor space. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Plot to Place – Designing Thriving and Resilient Neighborhoods]] | — |
| [[Hyperscale data centres skip Czechia for lack of power]] | Data centres count as an industrial use but are not yet tracked in the sector's shared research data. Enterprise-scale facilities need to sit near population centres; hyperscale ones are not coming to Czechia because the energy supply isn't there. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Why CEE is the Hottest Location in Europe for supply chain, logistics and industrial Development]] | [[Katrin Rybinskiy]], [[Julia Godard Lombard]] |
| [[I don't want to be too romantic about it]] | She interrupts her own praise to insist the building is a logistics centre with a client programme: traffic and goods flow must work. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | — |
| [[If cities don't plan for logistics, logistics takes space]] | Urban consolidation centres need land inside or at the edge of the city; failure to zone for it does not make the demand disappear, it makes it appropriate space opportunistically and inefficiently. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Integrated Urban Logistics – From last-mile to mobility hubs]] | [[Urban integration of logistics buildings]] |
| [[In a data centre the embodied carbon is in the pipes]] | For a typical office, a bit more than half of embodied carbon sits in sub- and superstructure. For a data centre the building fabric is "not that big of a concern" — the mechanical, electrical and plumbing systems dominate, which means the levers sit with equipment manufacturers, not just architects. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Cloud in the city – data centres, urban development and implications]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Embodied carbon intensity]] |
| [[In the forest you must be different; in the city you can match]] | A factory in a forest has to look completely different; this one, sitting beside panel housing, "looks the same" and therefore makes no violent attraction. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Challenge the perception of industrial architecture as 'boxing']] | [[Visual impact and acceptance by neighbours]] |
| [[In-city logistics is a separate typology with separate rules]] | Urban demand is served by "small business units" (SBUs) — pharmaceutical, food, one-hour delivery — sited on brownfields, commanding much higher rents on shorter leases, increasingly let to family businesses and e-commerce start-ups. Some developers call them "sticky boxes." | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Why CEE is the Hottest Location in Europe for supply chain, logistics and industrial Development]] | [[Urban integration of logistics buildings]], [[Lindsay Daphne Macuja]] |
| [[Industrial sheds as 4th-generation district heating nodes]] | With coal-based first-generation district heating closing, industrial buildings — data centres above all — are positioned as crucial waste-heat sources for 4th and 5th generation district heating and cooling networks now being re-mapped city by city. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Model to Reality – Sustainability in Industrial Buildings]] | [[Waste heat and cooling of data centres]], [[Waste heat recovery]] |
| [[Innovation blocked by missing regulation, not missing technology]] | Regulation for a four-wheel electric scooter did not yet exist in all the countries where Green Dash operated: new vehicle types require the regulatory category to be created in parallel. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Integrated Urban Logistics – From last-mile to mobility hubs]] | — |
| [[Innovations fail from missing support, not bad ideas]] | The mid-stage (TRL 5–7) real-world testing phase is where most solutions die — pilots demand city access, stakeholder coordination and money — and even proven TRL 8–9 solutions struggle to scale because each city has different regulations, infrastructure and stakeholders. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Integrated Urban Logistics – From last-mile to mobility hubs]] | — |
| [[Insulation was solved conventionally]] | Prvok's walls were printed as an inner and outer leaf with sprayed foam insulation in the cavity, giving thermal characteristics comparable to a regular contemporary Czech house. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Printing Against Gravity – From the construction site to the research lab]] | — |
| [[Investment agencies as a competitive instrument]] | Agencies such as CzechInvest exist to attract new business with tax benefits, labour support and permitting help; he rates CEE as doing infrastructure and incentive support markedly better than Western Europe. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Why CEE is the Hottest Location in Europe for supply chain, logistics and industrial Development]] | — |
| [[Investment is a EUR 40bn market, three-quarters European]] | European logistics investment ran at about EUR 40bn in 2025 (down ~3%), against a peak of ~EUR 60bn. Roughly EUR 30bn of the 40 is domestic or intra-European — unusually high versus the normal 60–65% — with almost nothing from Asia, the Middle East or Africa. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Performing Despite the Headwinds – Understanding the drivers of European logistics performance]] | — |
| [[Investor speed was a design variable]] | The first North Harbour neighbourhood was delivered within eight years; investors' ability to move fast is credited as part of the transformation. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Plot to Place – Designing Thriving and Resilient Neighborhoods]] | — |
| [[Investors chase tenant credit and forget the location]] | Assets bought for long e-commerce leases are now being re-examined: buyers over-weighted tenant credit and under-weighted where the building stood. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Performing Despite the Headwinds – Understanding the drivers of European logistics performance]] | — |
| [[Investors hunt the cheapest credits]] | When an investor pursues certification to obtain better banking conditions or resale terms rather than to occupy the building, the behaviour changes: the search is explicitly for the easiest available credits. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Criteria for a Green Building – Beyond energy performance LEED - BREEAM - WELL]] | — |
| [[Island methods travel badly to Central Europe]] | BREEAM's approach to landscape reflects England: a small island without large expanses of nature. DGNB, developed in Central Europe, embeds different landscape assumptions. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Logistics Development in the concept of certification and LCA]] | — |
| [[Italy's warehouses eat 432 hectares a year]] | ISPRA's 2025 land-consumption report records Italy artificialising 2.7 m² per second, 83.7 km² in 2024 — the worst year in twelve. Logistics areas alone grew by 432 hectares in that year (Emilia-Romagna +107, Piedmont +74, Lombardy +69). Cumulatively, logistics consumed 5,104 hectares between 2006 and 2022, and in 2022 accounted for 7% of Italy's entire net land consumption. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-external">External</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Renewable Matter on ISPRA 2025]] | [[Land take of logistics buildings]], [[Land take]] |
| [[Keep every drop on site]] | Stormwater strategy: nothing leaves the locality, even where legislation would allow it. On a site this size that means thousands of cubic metres — handled through a cascade of four ponds (sediment, stabilisation, then infiltration), and only then technical solutions such as injection wells. "Start with nature and then go with it." | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Challenge the perception of industrial architecture as 'boxing']] | [[Stormwater and sealed surfaces on logistics sites]], [[Stormwater runoff reduction]], [[Stormwater retention volume]] |
| [[KPIs as client-facing argument, not paperwork]] | Data and KPI analysis are described as a way to enforce design excellence internally and open dialogue with clients. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Henning Larsen Playbook for Biobased Buildings]] | — |
| [[Landlords want frictional vacancy on purpose]] | Investors would ideally hold 97–98% occupancy rather than 100%, because a little "frictional vacancy" is what lets existing customers expand in place. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Performing Despite the Headwinds – Understanding the drivers of European logistics performance]] | — |
| [[Landscape architecture's field has been rewritten]] | Over the last 10–20 years the discipline expanded from parks, playgrounds and urban realm into climate adaptation, rainwater management, biodiversity strategy and green infrastructure — a shift she credits partly to European regulation. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | — |
| [[Landscape drove the mobility plan, not vice versa]] | Breaking the runway into small neighbourhoods separated by green fingers and converting the runway into public space gives a maximum five-minute walk to a park; green access within ~300 m is cited as a strong liveability driver, and the landscape strategy shaped the 15-minute framework. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Plot to Place – Designing Thriving and Resilient Neighborhoods]] | — |
| [[Last mile is shortest but costliest]] | The final delivery leg represents 40–60% of total delivery cost while being the physically shortest segment of the chain. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-disputed">Disputed</span> | [[Integrated Urban Logistics – From last-mile to mobility hubs]] | [[Last-mile delivery emissions and traffic]], [[Last-mile delivery cost]] |
| [[Lease cycles make the market a slow-moving vessel]] | Because tenants are locked in, even a violent shock takes years to change the built landscape. Consumers feel price effects in months; the industry adjusts in two to three years. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Why CEE is the Hottest Location in Europe for supply chain, logistics and industrial Development]] | — |
| [[Lease length is the hidden design brief]] | Logistics tenants sign 5–7 year leases; manufacturers sign 15, and increasingly 20–25 years, because relocation costs are prohibitive. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Why CEE is the Hottest Location in Europe for supply chain, logistics and industrial Development]] | [[Adaptable and relocatable logistics structures]], [[Amy Naprstek]] |
| [[LEED supplies the ceiling the code lacks]] | Where the national standard has no upper bound, LEED's Useful Daylight Illuminance (work surface, around a 3,000 lux upper limit) and Annual Sunlight Exposure (floor-measured, 1,000 lux exceeded for more than 250 hours a year, expressed as a percentage of area) do — and ASE counts only areas actually intended for office work. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Criteria for a Green Building – Beyond energy performance LEED - BREEAM - WELL]] | [[Worker comfort and well-being]], [[Daylight autonomy]] |
| [[Less than 2% of England's offset land has sold]] | A year and a half into mandatory 10% Biodiversity Net Gain, 112 registered off-site projects promised over 4,000 hectares of habitat but under 2% of that registered land had actually been sold to developers by June 2025. On-site gains judged "non-significant" require no legal agreement and appear on no public register. The statutory habitat metric "has been demonstrated to be an ineffective proxy for wider biodiversity outcomes", and developers favour fast, easy habitats — grassland — over woodland and wetland. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-external">External</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Leverhulme Centre for Nature Recovery, University of Oxford, 2025]] | [[Biodiversity and ecological connectivity on logistics sites]], [[Biodiversity net gain]] |
| [[Lessons-learned list for lowering carbon]] | Prefer renovation over new build and demolition; optimise usable floor area (four people in 150, 100 or 50 m²); design for resilience, adaptability and flexibility; design for disassembly and reuse; use renewable energy and minimise consumption; prefer materials with EPDs and natural/local materials; ensure the longest possible service life — with the caveat that building only from wood is not always the right answer. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Logistics Development in the concept of certification and LCA]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Design for disassembly score]], [[Space utilisation]] |
| [[Lifts and PVs eat a quarter of the carbon budget]] | Elevators and photovoltaics — of which the building has comparatively little — together account for almost a quarter of total embodied carbon, and are where product-specific selection delivered the biggest gains. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Operational energy and on-site renewables]] |
| [[Liquid cooling may quietly defuse the water fight]] | As IT density rises, air-based cooling becomes impractical and liquid cooling takes over; pushed to its conclusion that means a closed loop filled once at opening, so consumption falls. He presents the water controversy as partly a misunderstanding of a moving technology — while conceding it is still a real problem where traditional cooling persists. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Cloud in the city – data centres, urban development and implications]] | [[Waste heat and cooling of data centres]], [[Water consumption]], [[Robin Jesenský]] |
| [[Lizard habitat in a parking lot]] | Many Czech warehouses install lizard habitats because they are cheap and easy — a pile of stones placed anywhere scores points. Whether such a habitat in the middle of a parking lot does anything for lizards is a separate, unasked question. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Logistics Development in the concept of certification and LCA]] | [[Biodiversity and ecological connectivity on logistics sites]], [[Ecological connectivity]] |
| [[Location is king, and it moves with the market]] | A centre-of-gravity exercise: a warehouse serving all of Europe sits in mid-Germany; narrow the market to Germany alone and it shifts east; add Austria and Switzerland and it lands near the Cheb/Karlovy Vary border; add Eastern Europe and it sits squarely in West Bohemia. The dot map of Czech warehouse density is the physical residue of that calculation. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Why CEE is the Hottest Location in Europe for supply chain, logistics and industrial Development]] | [[Marek Nový]] |
| [[Location quotient as a design-relevant lens]] | Sector concentration in a city vs the national average (above 1 = specialised) shows European cities specialise in ICT, financial, professional and administrative services, while manufacturing and logistics are *under*-represented inside cities. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Geography of Growth – Cities, AI, and the future of property]] | — |
| [[Logistics builds in a year; offices take four or five]] | Because logistics construction is so fast, the supply pipeline is only visible about 12 months out — beyond that, forecasting is "pretty tricky and actually probably meaningless." | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Performing Despite the Headwinds – Understanding the drivers of European logistics performance]] | — |
| [[Logistics centres cannot repack — so returns are destroyed]] | E-commerce centres have no repackaging capacity: once a bag is opened the item cannot re-enter stock, so returns go to outlets or are destroyed. Some centres lack the resource even to inspect returns, and consumers exploit this by returning empty boxes. He suggests repacking, or a shop for returned goods, as possible programmes. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | — |
| [[Logistics is architecture built to disappear]] | Logistics sits outside the city centre, tied to a highway, and is therefore not looked at; the brief reduces to cheap, fast, operational. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Build Your Own Playground – Architecture where no one looks for it]] | [[Urban integration of logistics buildings]] |
| [[Logistics is not one typology — it's a carbon spectrum]] | Some logistics buildings are completely dominated by embodied carbon because operational carbon is near-zero; at the other end, data centres have operational carbon that "completely eclipses" embodied. The designer's first job is to identify which driver dominates before optimising anything. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Net Zero That Holds Up – Whole-Life Carbon, Design Choices, and Delivery]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Whole-life carbon]] |
| [[Logistics lives in the sprawl, not the city]] | Transport and storage is less concentrated in major cities than nationally; the activity forms rings of urban sprawl *around* cities rather than within them — including around Prague. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Geography of Growth – Cities, AI, and the future of property]] | [[Urban integration of logistics buildings]], [[Ilinca Maria Baciu]] |
| [[Logistics takes 2% of the land and 10% of the jobs]] | France's binding "zéro artificialisation nette" trajectory has become the sector's central planning fight. The industry's own defence: transport and warehousing accounted for just 2% of new land consumption in 2012–2021 while supplying nearly 10% of salaried employment, and 50% of new warehouses are already built on brownfield (*friches*). Their counter-warning: pushing sheds away from demand centres lengthens truck kilometres. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-external">External</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[France Logistique, ZAN et logistique, 2025]] | [[Land take of logistics buildings]], [[Land take]], [[Jobs created]] |
| [[Long-life loose-fit and design for disassembly]] | Longevity, adaptability and flexibility as a "long life loop": these buildings arrive essentially as Lego, so the design question is how the elements are *connected* — no gluing, no welding, joints that can be undone. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Challenge the perception of industrial architecture as 'boxing']] | [[Adaptable and relocatable logistics structures]], [[Design for disassembly score]], [[Amy Naprstek]] |
| [[Low-carbon concrete has a scaling filter]] | Best-case concrete could move from ~0.12 to perhaps 0.05–0.06 kgCO2/kg today, but many clever concrete chemistries cannot be built by real contractors and are therefore a waste of time. He maintains a "concrete technology tracker." | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Net Zero That Holds Up – Whole-Life Carbon, Design Choices, and Delivery]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Embodied carbon intensity]] |
| [[Madrid's 15,000-home annual gap]] | From 2008 to 2024 Madrid completed on average around 20,600 homes a year while new household formation averaged just under 36,000. Copenhagen and Oslo show the same pattern; Helsinki is the counter-example, with completions outpacing household formation in 2017–2021 and house prices growing more slowly than the EU average. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Geography of Growth – Cities, AI, and the future of property]] | — |
| [[Making it look fancy is not sustainable architecture]] | Their earlier office work is disowned as surface treatment. Sustainable practice, in their revised definition, means understanding how people move in a community and building space that survives the sector's real cycle — a ~10-year lease after which fit-outs are ripped out and rebuilt. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Build Your Own Playground – Architecture where no one looks for it]] | — |
| [[Mandatory site greenery as anti-landscape policy]] | Czech industrial sites must keep 20–30% green, which he argues consumes ~30% more land than the same building would need in Germany or the US, pushes development onto open land outside the city, and produces greenery of no ecological value — including a demand to green a site that was formerly all concrete steel mill. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Industrial Architecture as Living Infrastructure – Why future design is defined by systems, data, and adaptability, not just form]] | [[Land take of logistics buildings]], [[Biodiversity and ecological connectivity on logistics sites]], [[Green area ratio]], [[Land take]] |
| [[Manufacturers and designers want opposite things]] | Every manufacturer prefers the simplest possible object, which has the highest chance of printing successfully, while architects want to express design freedom; the two expectations do not meet. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Printing Against Gravity – From the construction site to the research lab]] | — |
| [[Manufacturing is a better neighbour than logistics]] | Manufacturing generates fewer truck movements, better-paid jobs and longer tenure. Czech deal mix is unusual: more than half of last year's Czech deals were manufacturing, against only 21% for Europe as a whole (3PL 43%, retail ~20% Europe-wide). | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Why CEE is the Hottest Location in Europe for supply chain, logistics and industrial Development]] | [[Visual impact and acceptance by neighbours]], [[Jobs created]] |
| [[Manufacturing knowledge upstream of form]] | For the World of Volvo, the glulam manufacturer (Wiehag, Austria) was engaged early so the design was informed by how the timber could actually be produced. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Henning Larsen Playbook for Biobased Buildings]] | — |
| [[Manufacturing take-up more than doubled]] | Manufacturing was ~12% of European take-up in 2021 and has more than doubled its share by 2025, while retail and 3PL stayed flat. Presented as the best quantitative proxy for near-shoring actually happening. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-disputed">Disputed</span> | [[Performing Despite the Headwinds – Understanding the drivers of European logistics performance]] | — |
| [[Market windows open and close]] | Office design in Prague is described as a window that opened around 2012, peaked in 2021 on startup money, and has now closed with investment drying up — positioning is as much timing as skill. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Build Your Own Playground – Architecture where no one looks for it]] | — |
| [[Mass timber sites – tents, sealed containers, and a winter-open building]] | Weather protection on timber sites is handled with a full tent over the works or by delivering sealed containerised prefab elements. Denmark's largest timber building was left open through winter and still finished fine — the moisture panic is overstated. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Henning Larsen Playbook for Biobased Buildings]] | — |
| [[Master plans must evolve or the investment leaves]] | The master plans need to evolve with this if they want to attract these investments. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Why CEE is the Hottest Location in Europe for supply chain, logistics and industrial Development]] | — |
| [[Materials that local trades already know how to repair]] | Timber and straw are legible: anyone understands that wet hay gets pulled out and replaced, while a failed rubber gasket in an aluminium window produces a sense of alienation. He suggests that choosing materials could also mean considering the building trades a region knows, such as bricklaying. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | — |
| [[Mayors get trucks, not taxes]] | Unlike Germany, Czech municipalities capture very little tax or development contribution from industrial buildings, so local governments have almost no incentive to approve them. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Why CEE is the Hottest Location in Europe for supply chain, logistics and industrial Development]] | [[Visual impact and acceptance by neighbours]], [[Social acceptance]] |
| [[Measure it or you're just claiming it]] | Commitment to quantifiable nature-positive outcomes is what separates real ecological performance from a green facade as marketing. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | [[Biodiversity net gain]] |
| [[Melanopic light keeps winter workers awake]] | Beyond photopic lux, the vertical eye-plane melanopic value governs circadian response; in winter months, when natural exposure is low, untreated lighting leaves occupants sleepy, and artificial light has to be specified to compensate. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Criteria for a Green Building – Beyond energy performance LEED - BREEAM - WELL]] | [[Worker comfort and well-being]] |
| [[Micro data centres – who takes the waste]] | Responding to the mini-data-centres-next-to-houses idea (and a story about a man renting out part of his garden to a data centre company in exchange for free heat), he is open to the model but flags security control over critical infrastructure and end-of-life responsibility once the unit is obsolete after two or three years. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Cloud in the city – data centres, urban development and implications]] | [[Waste heat and cooling of data centres]] |
| [[Microclimate decides whether a masterplan works]] | Whether a comfortable microclimate can be delivered determines whether the neighbourhood is desirable at all. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Plot to Place – Designing Thriving and Resilient Neighborhoods]] | [[Outdoor microclimate around logistics buildings]] |
| [[Minimal surfaces graded by force flow]] | A published workflow fitted triply periodic minimal surfaces into a modelled load network for a bridge: the diamond variant performed best on overhangs, and the cell step size was scaled — denser where forces concentrate, coarser where they do not. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Printing Against Gravity – From the construction site to the research lab]] | — |
| [[Minus 65% carbon on the structure]] | The first all-CLT Penny market in Czechia (2021/2022), at the time the largest CLT structure in the country, delivered a 65% reduction in the carbon footprint of the construction; three are built with five or six expected. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Build Your Own Playground – Architecture where no one looks for it]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Embodied carbon intensity]] |
| [[Mission critical rewrites the brief]] | "Mission critical" means the building itself is load-bearing for financial transactions, hospitals and even the power system, so it needs permanent on-site operation and maintenance. A warehouse of dry goods can survive a power cut; this cannot. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Cloud in the city – data centres, urban development and implications]] | — |
| [[Modularity as a design-process tool]] | Once you decide the building is modular, you can react to "make it longer, wider" during the years of design without changing the system. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Challenge the perception of industrial architecture as 'boxing']] | — |
| [[Morally obsolete before technically obsolete]] | Buildings may become obsolete morally before they fail technically; the offices being built now will likely need a facade refurbishment in 30–40 years purely to meet the moral criteria of a prime building. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Challenge the perception of industrial architecture as 'boxing']] | [[Adaptable and relocatable logistics structures]] |
| [[More glass does not mean more daylight]] | Křečková opens on the persistent myth that daylight is solved by increasing the glazed proportion of the facade; in reality the thermal comfort penalty is severe, and daylight and overheating must be optimised as two simultaneous criteria. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Criteria for a Green Building – Beyond energy performance LEED - BREEAM - WELL]] | [[Worker comfort and well-being]], [[Thermal comfort]], [[Daylight autonomy]] |
| [[Most architecture is designed by nobodies]] | Most architecture that surrounds us is designed by people nobody knows. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Build Your Own Playground – Architecture where no one looks for it]] | — |
| [[Most of them are already urban]] | The majority of data centres sit in urban or suburban locations, or on city outskirts. The asset base is also hard to track because of confidentiality and because some announced projects are abandoned. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Cloud in the city – data centres, urban development and implications]] | [[Urban integration of logistics buildings]] |
| [[Multi-storey manufacturing is a Czech taboo]] | Building manufacturing in layers is "very new in Czech Republic", because the default is one vast floor with no gaps or incisions so the process is unbothered by the outside. The client explicitly asked for the opposite. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Challenge the perception of industrial architecture as 'boxing']] | [[Land take of logistics buildings]] |
| [[Multimodal siting – sea, rail, road, air]] | Beyond proximity to northwest-European urban clusters, a small container terminal along the dyke brings goods in by boat, with highway, rail and an airport at Lelystad within reach. The inbound half of the building is markedly smaller than the outbound half as a result. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | — |
| [[Municipalities get 3% — so they don't want you]] | Corporate income tax, personal income tax and social contributions all flow to the national level; the municipality's only local income is property tax, roughly 3% of total public proceeds. German municipalities by contrast assemble land and pick the businesses that will fund them for 20–30 years. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Industrial Architecture as Living Infrastructure – Why future design is defined by systems, data, and adaptability, not just form]] | — |
| [[Mycelium pavilion that returns to the field]] | For Milan Design Week the studio grew mycelium components in a Politecnico lab next to the site and assembled them into a demountable scaffolding system: the scaffolding returns to a building site and the mycelium goes back onto the field. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Henning Larsen Playbook for Biobased Buildings]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Design for disassembly score]] |
| [[Named toolchain, phase by phase]] | Conceptual/competition work runs in Rhino + Grasshopper with Ladybug/Honeybee; transition to production runs through Rhino.Inside Revit; expert groups exist separately for the "Rhino universe" and the "integrated/Revit universe". | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Henning Larsen Playbook for Biobased Buildings]] | — |
| [[Nature as the brief, not the garnish]] | "Nature first" means every project begins by gathering ecological intelligence on soil, water, climate and biodiversity, and lets that sharpen the design strategy. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | — |
| [[Nature branding became infrastructure policy]] | Singapore's 1990s government adopted "city in the gardens" initially as a brand slogan, then converted it into an infrastructural regime, including legislation requiring greenery in every built project. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | — |
| [[Nature designed to exclude people]] | Large areas were deliberately made inaccessible; limiting human pressure improves conditions for butterflies and insects, with a boardwalk and a few small access points. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | [[Public access and community use of logistics sites]], [[Biodiversity and ecological connectivity on logistics sites]], [[Publicly accessible area]] |
| [[Nature is the last thing built, and the money runs out]] | Good ecological ambitions are often a struggle in execution, because landscape is sequenced after the building and the budget is often exhausted by then. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | — |
| [[Nature-based solutions because pipes can't cope]] | Nature-based stormwater design is necessary: the volumes arriving in storm events now exceed what classical infrastructure can handle. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | [[Stormwater and sealed surfaces on logistics sites]] |
| [[Netherlands has had carbon limits since 2018]] | On the EU map of readiness, the leaders have had building carbon-footprint limits since 2022, the Netherlands since 2018, with databases and calculation tools already in place. Czechia is mid-pack: legislation and a new database, tools still in development. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Logistics Development in the concept of certification and LCA]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]] |
| [[New-build LEED never expires]] | LEED for new construction and interiors carries no expiry date; only LEED for Operation & Maintenance and WELL require recertification, on a three-year cycle. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Criteria for a Green Building – Beyond energy performance LEED - BREEAM - WELL]] | — |

She is explicit that no community engagement was involved yet. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Plot to Place – Designing Thriving and Resilient Neighborhoods]] | — |
