| [[No IKEA parts fit a printed house]] | Because every surface is curved and deformed, standard shelving, wardrobes and cladding are unusable; floors, ceilings, shelves and counters all had to be measured and made manually on site. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Printing Against Gravity – From the construction site to the research lab]] | — |
| [[No islands — connectivity is the metric]] | Biodiversity work is not about creating isolated green patches; animals move to find food and habitat, so ecological corridors should link designed green to the surroundings. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | [[Biodiversity and ecological connectivity on logistics sites]], [[Ecological connectivity]], [[Ece Yasar]] |
| [[No rule against confetti greenery]] | Asked whether LEED prevents green area being scattered into meaningless fragments (strips between parking bays) rather than kept continuous for biodiversity, the speaker could not point to a contiguity requirement — only a hunch that a half-square-metre patch would not be counted. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Criteria for a Green Building – Beyond energy performance LEED - BREEAM - WELL]] | [[Biodiversity and ecological connectivity on logistics sites]], [[Ecological connectivity]] |
| [[Nobody agrees how much water they use]] | There is no industry consensus on data centre water consumption; volumes are on the scale of a city's use, and a lot of it is wasted. Water has become one of the most recent flashpoints, after years in which the argument was about power. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Cloud in the city – data centres, urban development and implications]] | [[Waste heat and cooling of data centres]], [[Water consumption]] |
| [[Nobody asks about embodied carbon in CEE]] | Tenants in Central and Eastern Europe have never raised embodied carbon with him; the certification push comes from developers chasing ratings and from UK/French investor pressure. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Why CEE is the Hottest Location in Europe for supply chain, logistics and industrial Development]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]] |
| [[Nobody buys conceptual-stage LCA]] | Asked whether they offer early-stage/parametric LCA: they did not, because no client wanted it. LCA contracts always arrive at detailed stage, driven by BREEAM requirements or financing. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Logistics Development in the concept of certification and LCA]] | — |
| [[Nobody knows if twice the compute means half the floor area]] | He has asked many people in the industry whether a doubling of chip performance halves the required square metres, and there is no answer. Buildings now being built may need half their area converted to something else in ten or fifteen years. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Cloud in the city – data centres, urban development and implications]] | [[Adaptable and relocatable logistics structures]], [[Adaptability]] |
| [[Noise – the logistics is the problem, not the building]] | The manufacturing process is mostly contained by the building; the loudest thing is the logistics. In a food factory the specific culprit is pumping flour and sugar from tankers into silos. Hierarchy of remedies: distance first, then soft materials and vegetation, then acoustic fences — and concentrate the noise in one place you can treat. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Challenge the perception of industrial architecture as 'boxing']] | [[Visual impact and acceptance by neighbours]], [[Noise level]], [[Distance to housing]] |
| [[Norms force oversized heat pumps]] | Czech norms compel designers to install heat-pump capacity the building demonstrably does not need. Blower-door and airtightness testing proves the lower demand, but unlike fire safety — where performance-based engineering is accepted in CZ — the energy norms will not accept evidence. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Industrial Architecture as Living Infrastructure – Why future design is defined by systems, data, and adaptability, not just form]] | [[Operational energy and on-site renewables]], [[Heating energy demand]] |
| [[Not having a strategy was the mistake]] | Years of working without a defined focus client or segment described as an outright error, recognised only recently. The reframing: stop pursuing a house style, start defining a segment. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Build Your Own Playground – Architecture where no one looks for it]] | — |
| [[Not one forest offset achieved no net loss]] | A global review of 32 studies across five countries — at least 300,000 hectares of offsets against 180,000 hectares of impacts, 48 policies or individual offsets — found only about a third reported achieving no net loss. Wetland offsets succeeded around half the time; of forest offsets, which are two-thirds of all offsetting worldwide, none demonstrated success. No avoided-loss offset was shown to achieve NNL. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-external">External</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[zu Ermgassen et al., Conservation Letters, 2019]] | [[Biodiversity and ecological connectivity on logistics sites]], [[Biodiversity net gain]] |
| [[Not recyclable, and the clock is always running]] | Asked directly whether the printed material is recyclable, the answer was no — which is why the research experiments were run in clay, which recycles easily. A collapse wastes several tons that must be shovelled, cured and disposed of, and additives such as small plastic fibres make it a mixed material; once mixed, roughly ten minutes remain before it sets inside hose, mixer and pump. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Printing Against Gravity – From the construction site to the research lab]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]] |
| [[Offsets have no place in building design]] | He rejects credits and offsetting at building level outright: unnecessary, confusing, and dangerous because it would become cheaper to buy your way out than to design well. He returns to Dieste — if you can buy your way out of a constraint, that building never gets invented. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Net Zero That Holds Up – Whole-Life Carbon, Design Choices, and Delivery]] | — |
| [[Old sheds are becoming unlettable]] | European logistics is bifurcating: over 80% of take-up in Italy and the UK is now in modern stock, over 80% of vacant space in the Netherlands is second-hand, and UK assets built before the 2000s have seen negative net absorption since the pandemic. Older stock struggles to meet modern requirements and "retrofitting is often impractical due to structural limitations." | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-external">External</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[CBRE Investment Management, European Logistics – From Obsolescence to…]] | [[Adaptable and relocatable logistics structures]], [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]] |
| [[Old sheds knew things the new ones forgot]] | The older Kladno industrial buildings used natural/stack ventilation, daylight modulation and structural mass; today's simplified shed geometry throws that passive potential away. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Model to Reality – Sustainability in Industrial Buildings]] | [[Operational energy and on-site renewables]], [[Natural ventilation potential]], [[Daylight autonomy]] |
| [[Omnichannel and 3PL, not online, are 60% of demand]] | Omnichannel retailers, 3PLs and post/parcel operators together account for roughly 60% of take-up; traditional users take another 15–20%. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-disputed">Disputed</span> | [[Performing Despite the Headwinds – Understanding the drivers of European logistics performance]] | — |
| [[One hour is the basic unit of an architect]] | From roughly 25 to 65 is about 10,000 days and 80,000 hours. Putting a price on the hour gives an exact lifetime figure — but getting anyone to pay that price requires defining yourself as a problem solver rather than a designer. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Build Your Own Playground – Architecture where no one looks for it]] | — |
| [[Only 20-year warranties, whatever the material]] | 20 years is what contractors will give, no different for timber than concrete. Cladding may need partial replacement after 20 years, but the frame, kept dry, can last indefinitely — like the 200–300-year-old timber-slab warehouses in central Copenhagen. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]] |
| [[Only 20–25% built speculatively]] | Of roughly 2,000,000 m² developed in CZ, only about a fifth to a quarter went up without a signed occupier. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Industrial Architecture as Living Infrastructure – Why future design is defined by systems, data, and adaptability, not just form]] | — |
| [[Open Details – giving away the drawings]] | A Ramboll Foundation-funded research project publishes Henning Larsen's 1:5 construction details, including biogenic-material assemblies, on a free platform for download and reuse — an explicit attempt to push low-carbon solutions out into the industry. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Henning Larsen Playbook for Biobased Buildings]] | — |
| [[Operational carbon collapses, embodied carbon takes over]] | Because operational energy is falling so fast across the portfolio, the carbon locked into materials becomes the dominant term. The last decade was the energy decade; the next is the embodied carbon decade — across all typologies in the region. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Model to Reality – Sustainability in Industrial Buildings]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Whole-life carbon]], [[Operational carbon]] |
| [[Optimise a dream, don't optimise a compromise]] | Cost-first design lays "trip wires" across the process. Sell the ambition first, then spend the long tail of the project solving budget, fire and code against it. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | — |
| [[Ornamental to performative — the key-species trick]] | Asked how to move past decorative greenery: pick a key species (a bird, a larger mammal), build the storyline around it for client and community, and the species connected to it follow. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | [[Biodiversity and ecological connectivity on logistics sites]] |
| [[Over-build the height, don't charge for it]] | When a tenant asks for 8 m clear height, Panattoni builds 10 m and absorbs the cost, because constraining height forecloses future uses. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Industrial Architecture as Living Infrastructure – Why future design is defined by systems, data, and adaptability, not just form]] | [[Adaptable and relocatable logistics structures]], [[Adaptability]] |
| [[Oversize the wires now]] | Knowing electricity demand for EVs will rise, you can strengthen the building's electrical infrastructure and wiring at design stage to make the asset ready for the future. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Beyond the Concrete – Designing Buildings That Last]] | [[Adaptable and relocatable logistics structures]] |
| [[Owner-occupier plus design-and-build explains the score]] | ČSOB SHQ reached 90 LEED v3 credits — sixth to eighth place in all of Europe — attributed primarily to two procurement facts: the investor was also the owner and end user, and it ran as a single design-and-build entity responsible for the whole process. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Criteria for a Green Building – Beyond energy performance LEED - BREEAM - WELL]] | — |
| [[Owners don't know where their own buildings are]] | Portfolio clients operate at such a high level that they sometimes do not know where individual buildings sit; what they want is a portfolio-level risk picture and a decision on whether to invest in mitigation. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Beyond the Concrete – Designing Buildings That Last]] | — |
| [[Ownership is the tenant's defence against the landlord's ratchet]] | Built-to-own warehouses are invisible in the lease data everyone reports, yet would, he expects, correlate strongly with manufacturing: a tenant who invests heavily in automation cannot afford to be re-priced at renewal, so buys to control the real estate and the location. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Why CEE is the Hottest Location in Europe for supply chain, logistics and industrial Development]] | — |
| [[Parking hidden inside a fauna corridor]] | Existing forest patches flanked the site with a gap the municipality's master plan already intended to link. The project's forest habitat wraps the car parks, simultaneously closing the fauna corridor and screening the least attractive part of the site. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | [[Biodiversity and ecological connectivity on logistics sites]], [[Visual impact and acceptance by neighbours]], [[Ecological connectivity]] |
| [[Pavement is the unsolved carbon problem]] | For logistics and industrial sites the dominant CO2 focus should be the pavement and external areas, not the building; asphalt has no real low-carbon alternative anywhere in the world, and the volume is enormous. Mycelium-bound paving with crushed-concrete aggregate is being trialled but cannot yet take heavy trucks. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Henning Larsen Playbook for Biobased Buildings]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Truck flow and yard efficiency]], [[Embodied carbon intensity]], [[Yard area per dock]], [[Hynek Hrabík]] |
| [[Per-kilogram intensity is the wrong question]] | Ranked as *systems* the story inverts: a concrete slab is high, steel-beam-and-slab still high, CLT the most efficient structural form. Intensity and usage must be read together. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Net Zero That Holds Up – Whole-Life Carbon, Design Choices, and Delivery]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Embodied carbon intensity]] |
| [[Permits are stretched, then sold empty]] | Because the customer is unknown at permitting time, the strategy is to push height, access and traffic allowances to the maximum the zoning and community will accept. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Industrial Architecture as Living Infrastructure – Why future design is defined by systems, data, and adaptability, not just form]] | [[Adaptable and relocatable logistics structures]] |
| [[Permitting runs on emotion, not data]] | The stated mission with the in-house technical lead is "myth busting": permitting and design decisions in CZ are emotion-based and unsupported by evidence. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Industrial Architecture as Living Infrastructure – Why future design is defined by systems, data, and adaptability, not just form]] | — |
| [[Permitting time is itself the developer's product]] | Getting a permit in CEE can take three to seven years (best case six months, worst four years for a Czech warehouse); once granted, it can add 20–50% to the land value. Developers run 10–20 person land-scouting teams and are paid only when a tenant moves in. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Why CEE is the Hottest Location in Europe for supply chain, logistics and industrial Development]] | — |
| [[Phasing over 20–50 years, while operating]] | The phasing must be fixed at the very beginning because later options foreclose: phase one has to fund the final picture, "temporary" uses last 20 years, and each phase must be built while the rest of the site keeps running. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Challenge the perception of industrial architecture as 'boxing']] | — |
| [[PhDs embedded in the studio, not the university]] | Denmark's industrial PhD model puts candidates on the company payroll with a university partner; Henning Larsen runs a stable of them on ecosystem services, biodiversity KPIs, additive manufacturing, transformation of existing stock, microclimate/future weather, acoustics and AI, plus 20–30 interns a year. One acoustics PhD produced facade prefab panels with acoustic absorption built in, and spun out a startup. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Henning Larsen Playbook for Biobased Buildings]] | — |
| [[Pilsen – zero available sites]] | In the country's second-largest industrial market, a request for a 40,000 m² facility cannot be answered by a single site with a guaranteed move-in date inside two years. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Industrial Architecture as Living Infrastructure – Why future design is defined by systems, data, and adaptability, not just form]] | — |
| [[Placemaking is where the money is made]] | Hardware produces an economic return, but hardware also produces a "place feeling", which generates societal value — local shops, community, jobs — which loops back and raises the developer's own return. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Plot to Place – Designing Thriving and Resilient Neighborhoods]] | — |
| [[Planar layers cluster stress to 250% utilisation]] | Force-flow analysis showed the top layer's weight spreads evenly through a non-planar sample, whereas planar layers form clusters with utilisation reaching about 250% — far beyond what the material can carry. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Printing Against Gravity – From the construction site to the research lab]] | [[Structural utilisation]] |
| [[Planting the felled forest back doesn't offset]] | Asked whether replanting trees taken from a site could be proven beneficial through LCA: offsetting is not accepted in official calculations, and even modelled as a scenario the sequestered carbon would be a very small number. The genuine benefits — biodiversity, green corridors — sit outside the carbon metric. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Logistics Development in the concept of certification and LCA]] | [[Biodiversity and ecological connectivity on logistics sites]], [[Carbon sequestration by planting]] |
| [[Power and labour, not land, are the real constraints]] | The historic constraints on logistics location have been power and labour rather than land; land is "normally surmountable." | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Performing Despite the Headwinds – Understanding the drivers of European logistics performance]] | — |
| [[Prague punches far above its weight]] | Prague holds roughly 10–15% of the Czech population but accounts for 25–30% of the national economy, with ICT alone just under 20% of Prague's GDP — a concentration credited for its office demand and productivity growth. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Geography of Growth – Cities, AI, and the future of property]] | — |
| [[Prague's pipes are an asset — sell the heat]] | District heating coverage is high in Northern and Eastern Europe and uncommon in Western and Southern Europe. Where a network already exists, the move is to pick plots near the pipes — or lay new pipe to reach them — recover the heat with a heat pump and sell it to the city's district heating company, as is routine in Finland, Denmark and Sweden. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Cloud in the city – data centres, urban development and implications]] | [[Waste heat and cooling of data centres]], [[Heat recovery radius]], [[Waste heat recovery]] |
| [[Predicting simulations instead of running them]] | Having run large volumes of daylight and microclimate simulations, the team is training on that output to predict results rather than simulate each case. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Henning Larsen Playbook for Biobased Buildings]] | — |
| [[Printed concrete weathers badly and must be sealed]] | It is porous, its layer cavities hold water and dust, and freeze-thaw can deteriorate it quickly; the practical response is wrapping during curing and then a hydrophobic paint or coating before shipping. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Printing Against Gravity – From the construction site to the research lab]] | — |
| [[Printed shell as lost formwork]] | Because interlayer bonding undermines the printed object's structural credibility, one workable strategy is to print a lost formwork and pour ordinary concrete inside, making the poured core load-bearing and the print merely the mould — which still removes conventional formwork. In Prvok, steel rebars were placed into small cavities and acted as columns. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Printing Against Gravity – From the construction site to the research lab]] | — |
| [[Printing is mostly shovelling, not coding]] | In practice concrete printing failure means tons of set material to be hammered, shovelled into boxes and landfilled. Sectional test prints of the Prvok geometry almost always collapsed. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Printing Against Gravity – From the construction site to the research lab]] | — |
| [[Production wants low, distribution wants high — for energy reasons]] | Standard warehouses are 12 m clear internally to suit racking; production tenants ask for only 6–8 m, partly because they don't need high racking and partly because heating and cooling a tall volume is expensive. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Why CEE is the Hottest Location in Europe for supply chain, logistics and industrial Development]] | [[Operational energy and on-site renewables]], [[Heating energy demand]] |
| [[Property is infrastructure, not real estate]] | We don't take our properties as really property, it's infrastructure for business and it's very important to look into it through this perspective. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Industrial Architecture as Living Infrastructure – Why future design is defined by systems, data, and adaptability, not just form]] | — |
| [[Rail freight has been stuck at 16.8% for years]] | Against an EU policy target of a 30% freight modal share by 2030, rail's share has stagnated at 16.8%. Every rail-served logistics scheme is being justified against a target the sector is nowhere near. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-external">External</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[International Railway Journal, 2023]] | [[Road freight impact]] |
| [[Rail is a rounding error – over 90% moves by truck]] | Multimodal terminals exist but serve heavy automotive and heavy industry, not consumer goods. Companies request rail, then quickly abandon it because so few rail terminals have warehousing attached. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Why CEE is the Hottest Location in Europe for supply chain, logistics and industrial Development]] | [[Road freight impact]] |
| [[Rain harvested to grow the walls]] | Roof rainwater irrigates the green façades and reduces load on the retention pools; the roof meadow itself absorbs water that would otherwise run off. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | [[Stormwater and sealed surfaces on logistics sites]], [[Water reuse share]], [[Stormwater runoff reduction]] |
| [[Rainwater is not process water]] | Using harvested rainwater in food machinery fails on purity: the debris requires an intense purification process. Wastewater heat recovery from the plant is suggested instead. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Challenge the perception of industrial architecture as 'boxing']] | [[Water reuse share]], [[Waste heat recovery]] |
| [[Ramboll's benchmark database, and its warehouse gap]] | Roughly 400–500 carbon assessments worldwide, with a public viewer called Compare. He admits the light-industrial/warehouse typology has comparatively few assessments in it, "because it's not as common that we would do it." | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Net Zero That Holds Up – Whole-Life Carbon, Design Choices, and Delivery]] | [[Embodied carbon intensity]] |
| [[RCP 8.5 called the most reasonably dirty one]] | 8.5 it is like, let's say business as usual, the most dirty one, let's say the most reasonably dirty one | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Beyond the Concrete – Designing Buildings That Last]] | — |
| [[Read the metadata or you don't know the number]] | The most important discipline in using LCA data is reading the metadata behind the figure — representativeness, system boundaries, how the value was aggregated, which EPDs and manufacturers fed it. In One Click LCA the derivation of generic datasets is sometimes not findable at all. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Logistics Development in the concept of certification and LCA]] | — |
| [[Recycled-PET envelope beats aluminium and concrete]] | The translucent panel system used (based on recycled PET/plastics) is presented as a better envelope option than aluminium, concrete or the alternatives. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Challenge the perception of industrial architecture as 'boxing']] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]] |
| [[Recycling can raise the carbon footprint]] | Reuse and recycling do not automatically reduce carbon. A thesis on recycled aggregate found the resulting concrete had a *higher* footprint than concrete from raw materials; a wall built from recycled concrete may need more concrete than one from primary material. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Logistics Development in the concept of certification and LCA]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Material reuse share]] |
| [[Reinforcement loses to nozzle collision]] | Ideally the robot would print a few layers and a rebar would be set upright into them, but the nozzle would then crash into the protruding steel on later passes — which is why printed structures cannot be properly reinforced and most projects post-tension after printing instead. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Printing Against Gravity – From the construction site to the research lab]] | — |
| [[Removing a traffic lane raised apartment prices 20–30%]] | Near Copenhagen's meatpacking district a two-lane road was cut to one with a pedestrian/recreational strip in the middle, against loud objections from residents and shopkeepers. Resale values subsequently rose 20–30% and businesses moved in — a case for prototyping and temporary interventions as the way past the fear of loss. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Henning Larsen Playbook for Biobased Buildings]] | — |
| [[Renewables as price insulation, not just carbon]] | Cities with high local renewable generation have pricing tied to domestic capacity and are less exposed to global oil and electricity spikes — making the Nordics and Spain competitive for AI investment. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Geography of Growth – Cities, AI, and the future of property]] | [[Electricity price]] |
| [[Renovating an office saved about 80%]] | A comparison of demolish-and-rebuild versus renovation for an administrative building showed savings of around 80% — but explicitly because the load-bearing structure, the most impactful material, was kept. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Logistics Development in the concept of certification and LCA]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Embodied carbon saved by reuse]], [[Thomas Olsen]] |
| [[Rents falling in CEE is good news, not bad]] | Headline warehouse rents in CEE are roughly stable or slightly falling year-on-year, against Munich around EUR 7–11/m²/month and London around EUR 27/m²/month. He reads softening rent as an attractor of investment and jobs. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Why CEE is the Hottest Location in Europe for supply chain, logistics and industrial Development]] | — |
| [[Rents grew 70% nominal but only 15% real in 16 years]] | Prime logistics rents hit unprecedented double-digit growth (15–16% at the 2022 peak) and are now at ~3.5%. But over 16 years nominal rents are up ~70% while real rents are up only ~15% — "not a terribly exciting performance in real terms." | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Performing Despite the Headwinds – Understanding the drivers of European logistics performance]] | — |
| [[Reshoring is not coming (contrarian)]] | Asked directly whether deglobalisation and onshoring will drive European logistics demand, he says the data shows no such movement: European production and energy costs would outweigh tariff savings, and peripheral regions where manufacturing sits have weak demographics and cannot supply labour. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Geography of Growth – Cities, AI, and the future of property]] | — |
| [[Residential and logistics cannot share a block; offices and retail can]] | Mixed use is rejected for housing on operational grounds — logistics runs 24/7 — but offices, retail and light industrial co-exist well; one Prague business park dedicates a third of its zone to retail, showrooms and a supermarket serving the surrounding community. The binding limit on industry inside cities remains road access. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Industrial Architecture as Living Infrastructure – Why future design is defined by systems, data, and adaptability, not just form]] | [[Urban integration of logistics buildings]], [[Mixed-use ratio]], [[Lindsay Daphne Macuja]] |
| [[Resilience is a second layer over sustainability]] | Sustainability assumes normal conditions; resilience asks whether the building survives the change in conditions and returns to normal operation. UCEEB is developing evaluation tools in an EU project: RB tool (resilient building) and RN tool (resilient neighbourhood), scoring hazard, exposure and vulnerability. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Logistics Development in the concept of certification and LCA]] | — |
| [[Restoration became law, and cities must not lose a tree]] | The EU Nature Restoration Regulation, in force since 18 August 2024, requires restoration of at least 20% of EU land and sea by 2030 and all ecosystems needing it by 2050, with member states bringing 30% of degraded habitats into good condition by 2030, 60% by 2040, 90% by 2050. It also mandates no net loss of national urban green space and urban tree canopy. Over 80% of European habitats are in poor condition. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-external">External</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[European Parliament, February 2024]] | [[Biodiversity and ecological connectivity on logistics sites]], [[Tree count and canopy cover]] |
| [[Retail warehousing exists because master plans forbid mixing people and trucks]] | Consumer access is not permitted on industrial plots, so a distinct "retail warehousing" subsector has emerged on highway junctions where zoning allows customers and private cars. The reasons are traffic and the safety hazard of mixing personal vehicles with trucks. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Why CEE is the Hottest Location in Europe for supply chain, logistics and industrial Development]] | [[Pedestrian–truck safety on logistics sites]], [[Public access and community use of logistics sites]], [[Adam Večeřa]], [[Julia Kopác]] |
| [[Retention ponds redesigned as habitat]] | Planning required retention capacity proportional to paved area. Rather than dry-then-full engineering basins, that mandated water volume was designed as wetland. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | [[Stormwater and sealed surfaces on logistics sites]], [[Biodiversity and ecological connectivity on logistics sites]], [[Stormwater retention volume]] |
| [[Rethink the logistics OF the city]] | Reframing the lecture series' own title: what changed in Copenhagen was the logistics of a city, not logistics as a building typology; the harbour front went from backside to front stage. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Plot to Place – Designing Thriving and Resilient Neighborhoods]] | — |
| [[Rethink the normalising unit per typology]] | kgCO2/m² is the default but not always useful: carbon per patient for a hospital, per m² or per tonne of storage for a warehouse, per MWh or per MW of capacity for a data centre. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Net Zero That Holds Up – Whole-Life Carbon, Design Choices, and Delivery]] | [[Carbon per pallet handled]], [[Carbon usage effectiveness]] |
| [[Rethinking the vehicle beats electrifying it]] | The Green Dash pilot (Latvia/Sweden, with Latvian Post and Riga City Council) did not electrify an existing van but redesigned the vehicle as a compact four-wheel electric cargo scooter, delivering a 30% efficiency gain versus traditional vans, reduced stop times and better working conditions. The same vehicle was tested by the city for street cleaning and park maintenance. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Integrated Urban Logistics – From last-mile to mobility hubs]] | [[Last-mile delivery emissions and traffic]] |
| [[Reuse can lose on whole-life carbon]] | Reusing an existing building saves upfront embodied carbon but often can't reach modern operational performance; on a whole-life basis the reuse can come out *higher*. That flips again if the energy supply is renewable enough. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Net Zero That Holds Up – Whole-Life Carbon, Design Choices, and Delivery]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Embodied carbon saved by reuse]], [[Whole-life carbon]], [[Julia Godard Lombard]], [[Katrin Rybinskiy]] |
| [[Risk analysis also finds the upside]] | Risk work is emotionally coded as negative, but the same analysis that shows one customer segment is no longer profitable may show that another has become viable. Transition risks are formally paired with transition *opportunities*. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Beyond the Concrete – Designing Buildings That Last]] | — |
| [[Risk management is not a risk manager]] | Companies delegate risk to a risk-management team, often parked inside legal, and then consider the problem solved. Even the most capable team cannot manage risk for a whole company; escalation paths and cross-team communication are the actual mechanism. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Beyond the Concrete – Designing Buildings That Last]] | — |
| [[Robot reach dictates architectural form]] | The industrial robot reaches roughly 3 metres with 6 axes, so Prvok's printable pieces became C-shaped segments — the most convenient shape around a fixed robot — and nothing much larger can be printed. Segments were printed in České Budějovice and trucked to Prague on a steel base frame, with transport vibration and cracking designed for. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Printing Against Gravity – From the construction site to the research lab]] | — |
| [[Robots don't need a view — which makes the box more modular]] | In 20-plus years these buildings will be largely robotised; daylight may still be wanted, but windows to look *out* of will not be. Removing the window question makes the envelope far more modular. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Challenge the perception of industrial architecture as 'boxing']] | [[Automation and operational efficiency of logistics]] |
| [[Roof overhangs as anti-light-pollution device]] | The concrete roof overhangs at ČSOB SHQ were credited with eliminating light smog by preventing light emitted from the building escaping directly upward. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Criteria for a Green Building – Beyond energy performance LEED - BREEAM - WELL]] | [[Visual impact and acceptance by neighbours]] |
| [[Rotate a 2D trick into a Gothic dome]] | Rotating the non-planar profiles around an axis and duplicating them five times produces a column-to-arch-to-dome structure, suggesting the method could deliver Gothic-like geometries rather than only extruded walls. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Printing Against Gravity – From the construction site to the research lab]] | — |
| [[Same building, five consultants, five answers]] | Financing is currently tied more to operational performance, and embodied-carbon-linked finance will stay difficult while market variation is this wide. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Net Zero That Holds Up – Whole-Life Carbon, Design Choices, and Delivery]] | [[Embodied carbon intensity]] |
| [[Same material, different path, opposite outcome]] | In controlled tests with identical geometry, material quantity, layer count and print time, the only variable being planar versus non-planar toolpath, the planar samples failed while the non-planar ones held their shape. Changing only the print path buys both design freedom and structural soundness at no material cost. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Printing Against Gravity – From the construction site to the research lab]] | [[Structural utilisation]] |
| [[Same rules for all materials, not subsidies for timber]] | In Germany and Austria there is effectively no price or standards difference between a concrete, steel or timber roof structure. The stated goal in working with the Czech standardisation agency is parity of rules across materials. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Model to Reality – Sustainability in Industrial Buildings]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]] |
| [[Scores converge, emphases don't]] | Across the same administrative buildings the final scores from different systems come out broadly similar, but the systems weight different things: LEED looks more at people, BREEAM more at sustainability and energy. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Logistics Development in the concept of certification and LCA]] | — |
| [[Self-generated power as a development advantage]] | A developer holding a good location *plus* secured power — grid or self-generated — is in a materially stronger position; power has become another constraint on development for logistics. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Performing Despite the Headwinds – Understanding the drivers of European logistics performance]] | [[Operational energy and on-site renewables]] |
| [[Sell the warehouse as a power station]] | Friedrich reframes the pitch to mayors: nearly every German warehouse now carries rooftop PV, and warehouses — data centres in particular — generate large volumes of waste heat that municipal heating plants can use. That is what has to be communicated. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Model to Reality – Sustainability in Industrial Buildings]] | [[Waste heat and cooling of data centres]], [[Waste heat recovery]], [[Rooftop PV generation]] |
| [[Service trips – the invisible fifth of traffic]] | Service logistics — technicians, maintenance crews, repair vehicles — is routinely overlooked but can account for 20–30% of commercial vehicle traffic in cities, and demands flexible access and parking. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Integrated Urban Logistics – From last-mile to mobility hubs]] | — |
| [[Seven-metre floors flatter the per-m² number]] | Because floor-to-floor heights average ~7 m, per-square-metre carbon intensity is not comparable to an office typology. Normalised to office-like ~4 m floors, the same building would land around 4–4.5. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Embodied carbon intensity]] |
| [[Shared warehouses could cut construction by 60–70%]] | Asked what unused potential exists, the answer is not a new building form but AI-and-app-enabled shared logistics centres — Penny and Nike in the same shed, packages routed to whichever slot is free. Estimated effect: a 60–70% drop in warehouse construction by using average existing space better. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Build Your Own Playground – Architecture where no one looks for it]] | [[Land take of logistics buildings]], [[Space utilisation]] |
| [[Ships are cheap, trucks are not]] | Road freight is extremely carbon intensive, while moving the same material by ship is "incredibly low carbon." | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Net Zero That Holds Up – Whole-Life Carbon, Design Choices, and Delivery]] | [[Road freight impact]] |
| [[Shrinking households multiply demand]] | Household sizes are falling across European cities, so more dwellings are needed even where population growth is modest. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Geography of Growth – Cities, AI, and the future of property]] | — |
| [[Silos are the recurring barrier]] | Sectoral silos between actors named as one of the most common barriers across urban mobility and beyond; EIT Urban Mobility's match-and-connect function — 250+ partners, 1200+ organisations, 420+ pilots in 185+ cities — is explicitly built to break them. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Integrated Urban Logistics – From last-mile to mobility hubs]] | — |
| [[Singapore's 20 km road becomes a green corridor]] | The North-South Corridor converts a roughly 20-kilometre asphalt arterial — currently a barrier between neighbourhoods — into an integrated green corridor supporting biodiversity and community alongside traffic. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | [[Ecological connectivity]] |
| [[Single-function land use is the real waste]] | On whether large logistics hubs belong in city centres: the answer is not location but function-mixing. If a facility must sit outside the city, it must carry additional functions. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Plot to Place – Designing Thriving and Resilient Neighborhoods]] | [[Land take of logistics buildings]], [[Mixed-use ratio]] |
| [[Single-picking is why e-commerce eats space]] | E-commerce needs far more space per unit of sales because items are picked singly rather than shipped in bulk, returns need their own space (often a separate building), and SKU counts are an order of magnitude higher — a supermarket holds ~60,000 products, a DIY retailer ~70,000, an online retailer half a million or more. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Performing Despite the Headwinds – Understanding the drivers of European logistics performance]] | [[Throughput per site area]] |
| [[Site lighting at logistics scale is a biodiversity problem]] | Because the surfaces are so vast, applying standard street lighting to a logistics site produces light pollution at a scale that becomes a serious biodiversity problem. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Logistics Development in the concept of certification and LCA]] | [[Biodiversity and ecological connectivity on logistics sites]] |
| [[Six segments, one scarce resource]] | Urban logistics is multiple overlapping systems, all competing for the same limited space — which makes it a space-allocation and governance challenge, not merely a transport problem. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Integrated Urban Logistics – From last-mile to mobility hubs]] | — |
| [[Six storeys of industry under 3,350 homes]] | SEGRO V-Park Grand Union in Alperton, London, completed 2024, is the UK's first multi-storey industrial development: 134,500 sq ft over six levels — two lower floors of loading bays and parking, four upper floors of industrial units — embedded in a mixed-use regeneration delivering 3,350 homes, 35% affordable, and up to 250 jobs. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-external">External</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[SEGRO, April 2024]] | [[Land take of logistics buildings]], [[Urban integration of logistics buildings]] |
| [[Skylights as a legislative contradiction]] | Czech roofs carry large areas of skylights that he says are poor for building energy performance and for the processes inside — regulation pulling in opposite directions. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Industrial Architecture as Living Infrastructure – Why future design is defined by systems, data, and adaptability, not just form]] | [[Operational energy and on-site renewables]], [[Daylight autonomy]] |
| [[Skylights as wayfinding in a 565 m building]] | At 565 m long and 80 m wide, navigation is itself a design problem: a central "street" runs the length of the terminal with branch roads, and a rhythm of skylights gives occupants a sense of position deep in the interior. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | [[Worker comfort and well-being]], [[Daylight autonomy]] |
| [[Slice resolution can be tuned locally, not globally]] | Rather than lowering layer height across a whole object to resolve one window, layer height and orientation can be adjusted locally. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Printing Against Gravity – From the construction site to the research lab]] | — |
| [[Soil is a biodiversity reservoir and a carbon bank]] | Millions of species live in soil and it stores carbon, yet it is routinely removed during construction; she openly admits she doesn't know how this is accounted for. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | [[Biodiversity and ecological connectivity on logistics sites]] |
| [[Soil study at 2,500 hectares, not at the plot line]] | With the Soil Science and Conservation Research Institute in Prague, Panattoni modelled roughly 2,500 hectares around the Kladno park — scenarios for how the buildings affect water behaviour and soil erosion, plus soil quality mapping. The finding he flags as striking: how poor the condition of Czech agricultural fields already is. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Model to Reality – Sustainability in Industrial Buildings]] | [[Stormwater and sealed surfaces on logistics sites]] |
| [[Solar is now a building code, not a choice]] | Under the recast EPBD, all new non-residential buildings over 250 m² must have solar installed by 31 December 2026 where suitable and feasible; existing non-residential buildings over 500 m² must follow by 31 December 2027 when undergoing major renovation or permitted roof works. From 29 May 2026 every new building must be designed "solar-ready" — orientation, roof design and structural capacity to carry future panels. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-external">External</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[European Commission, Solar energy in buildings (EPBD)]] | [[Operational energy and on-site renewables]], [[Structural utilisation]], [[Rooftop PV generation]] |
| [[Spain's housing crisis is six provinces]] | The much-protested Spanish affordability crisis is highly concentrated: roughly 6–7 provinces out of 50, all major urban cores. Spanish house prices rose about 10% in the last year against ~4% in the Eurozone, while "empty Spain" sees falling prices. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Geography of Growth – Cities, AI, and the future of property]] | — |
| [[Speak their language or be invited too late]] | if you don't start speaking their language, you will not be invited to the conversation. You will be invited too late. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Build Your Own Playground – Architecture where no one looks for it]] | — |
| [[Speculative development sits at ~38%]] | Normally 30–35% speculative versus committed pre-lets; currently ~38%, which he reads as a balanced market that has priced in the vacancy risk. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Performing Despite the Headwinds – Understanding the drivers of European logistics performance]] | — |
| [[Stacking is normal — everywhere but here]] | Multi-storey warehousing has existed for decades in Japan, South Korea, Singapore and Hong Kong, where the largest facility rises to the height of a 22-storey building. Europe and the US barely build it: above three storeys a one-hour fire rating forces heavy steel or concrete, urban road layouts can't take the lorry volumes, and rentable efficiency drops to about 75% once ramps, lifts and plant rooms are deducted. The first US ground-up multistorey — Prologis Georgetown Crossroads, Seattle, three levels, 590,000 sq ft — only opened in November 2018. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-external">External</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Transport Intelligence]] | [[Land take of logistics buildings]], [[Space utilisation]] |
| [[Stakeholders don't care what's around the city]] | Decision-makers are dispersed global stakeholders whose interest is the profit of the area, not what the development does to its surroundings. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Build Your Own Playground – Architecture where no one looks for it]] | [[Urban integration of logistics buildings]] |
| [[Stop making objects]] | Her standing instruction to the architects in the studio. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | — |
| [[Stored goods dictate where the building can go]] | Network design starts from SKU characteristics — what the product is made of, how it's packaged, how it's stored — and material flammability feeds back into fire design and therefore into permissible location. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Why CEE is the Hottest Location in Europe for supply chain, logistics and industrial Development]] | — |
| [[Straightened rivers lost their capacity to flood]] | Years of straightening rivers destroyed their flood capacity; returning natural meanders and space lets the river behave as a living system again. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | — |
| [[Straw façades cut truck movements fivefold]] | The compressed-straw (Ecococon) façade elements are so light that, per square metre of façade, delivering them took roughly one fifth the truck trips precast concrete sandwich panels would have. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]] |
| [[Structural stone – a knowledge problem, not a cost problem]] | Stone is near-zero-processing and returning as a structural material; France has built cost-effective limestone schools. Structural stone tolerates lower quality and coarser cutting than facade stone, and quarry spoil is cheap and structurally fine. Using stone can also delete the finishes layer entirely. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Net Zero That Holds Up – Whole-Life Carbon, Design Choices, and Delivery]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]] |
| [[Supply chains gained layers, and buildings gained variety]] | The old linear chain has fragmented into parallel models: cross-docks where trucks come in and vans go out, chains that skip the regional DC, direct-to-consumer from a single fulfilment centre. More models means more demand for space and a wider variety of building types. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Performing Despite the Headwinds – Understanding the drivers of European logistics performance]] | — |
| [[Support strategies don't scale down from plastic]] | Concrete supports are heavy, stick hard and must be hammered off; thin plastic-style support columns would collapse under concrete, and 3D support structures would consume large amounts of material. Alternatives are lightweight filler balls or printing inside a large box of sand — but sand is heavy enough to deform overhangs under its own weight, and removed fillers leave material stuck to the top layer. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Printing Against Gravity – From the construction site to the research lab]] | — |
| [[Sustainability arrives as a minimum, not an ambition]] | Developers install only the minimum EV charging needed to satisfy a BREEAM-type standard; more only if the tenant explicitly asks. He reports never being asked about electric or hydrogen trucks at all in CEE. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Why CEE is the Hottest Location in Europe for supply chain, logistics and industrial Development]] | — |
| [[Sustainability as risk elimination]] | Working with Amazon taught the company that their "unique approach to sustainable operation" is inseparable from being risk-free. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Industrial Architecture as Living Infrastructure – Why future design is defined by systems, data, and adaptability, not just form]] | — |
| [[Switzerland is digging a 500 km freight tunnel]] | Cargo Sous Terrain proposes an automated underground freight network from Geneva to St Gallen at 40 m depth, CHF 30–35 billion, entirely privately funded, with enabling legislation in force since August 2022. First section, Härkingen–Zurich (70 km), targeted for 2031; a ZHAW study estimates the system with bundled delivery could cut daily truck mileage in Zurich by a quarter. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-external">External</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[SWI swissinfo.ch]] | [[Last-mile delivery emissions and traffic]], [[Road freight impact]] |
| [[Tailoring is a one-way door]] | Buildings can be tailor-made "to the point where it's not usable for the future", and with Amazon or H&M investing multiples of the building's value in technology, the developer has no choice. Since the chance that another tenant uses such a building the same way after ~20 years is very limited, it must be possible to recycle it easily, ideally on site. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Industrial Architecture as Living Infrastructure – Why future design is defined by systems, data, and adaptability, not just form]] | [[Adaptable and relocatable logistics structures]], [[Design for disassembly score]] |
| [[Tech companies are buying BIM models with licences]] | Software vendors are approaching the studio offering free licences in exchange for its BIM models, because those models are training data for their industry. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Henning Larsen Playbook for Biobased Buildings]] | — |
| [[Tell the story in the right order]] | The skywalk was sequenced along the chronological axis of manufacturing — water, malt, then the process — so visitors do not start from the finished product. Two routes, public and guided, vertically stacked. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Challenge the perception of industrial architecture as 'boxing']] | [[Public access and community use of logistics sites]] |
| [[Ten thousand options instead of one]] | For the Fælledby neighbourhood, rather than developing a single scheme they generated and optimised across thousands of variants, tuning density upward while holding daylight stable. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Henning Larsen Playbook for Biobased Buildings]] | [[Daylight autonomy]] |
| [[Tenant demand follows a Gaussian curve]] | Industrial tenants ramp production to only 20–30% in the first two years, peak at about five years, then decline; the building's energy systems must be sized for the peak while running at 30–40% load in year one. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Model to Reality – Sustainability in Industrial Buildings]] | [[Operational energy and on-site renewables]] |
| [[Tested to 50 tons without failing]] | Prvok's printed structure was designed to norms for 5 tons from above (roof plus snow plus wind plus people on the roof, a scenario acknowledged as never happening). The test machine's 50-ton maximum was reached with the structure still holding and the load-deflection graph still rising. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Printing Against Gravity – From the construction site to the research lab]] | [[Structural utilisation]] |
| [[The asset class rotated three times in fifteen years]] | Logistics was unfundable around 2008–09; COVID and long e-commerce leases made it the diversification target in 2021–23; today hotels lead, and logistics attracts capital only with long leases — with defence emerging as a key industrial driver. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Industrial Architecture as Living Infrastructure – Why future design is defined by systems, data, and adaptability, not just form]] | — |
| [[The barista must afford to live there]] | The 15-minute neighbourhood is only honest if it covers workers as well as residents. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Plot to Place – Designing Thriving and Resilient Neighborhoods]] | — |
| [[The barrier is cultural, not technical]] | Asked for the biggest institutional obstacle to nature-core logistics, she names the capitalist system and design habit; European regulation, by contrast, has helped. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | — |
| [[The best logistics building he knows is a 1970s brick shed]] | Eladio Dieste's warehouse in Montevideo: post-tensioned Gaussian brick vaults, a new roof on much older walls, still functional. It gets its strength from form, not mass. Dieste designed it that way because Uruguay had limited access to cement and steel — constraint forced the innovation. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-disputed">Disputed</span> | [[Net Zero That Holds Up – Whole-Life Carbon, Design Choices, and Delivery]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]] |
| [[The best-certified warehouse still looks like a box]] | Two Czech examples — the highest-rated logistics building in the country and the largest logistics park near Pilsen — are shown next to a conventional warehouse; visually there is no difference. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Logistics Development in the concept of certification and LCA]] | [[Visual impact and acceptance by neighbours]] |
| [[The bigger the data centre, the bigger the target]] | Concentration is a security and resilience liability in a world with war in it, so a network of smaller facilities is systemically more robust than one or two giants. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Cloud in the city – data centres, urban development and implications]] | — |
| [[The biggest possible hall reflex is a carbon cost]] | Longer spans mean bigger beams; accepting shorter spans shrinks the structure. The level of flexibility built into a logistics building has to be balanced against it. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Adaptable and relocatable logistics structures]] |
| [[The budget cut became the spatial idea]] | The double-height, two-storey office with steel bridges came from omitting floor structure over part of the plan under a very tight budget; the saving on floor panels produced the volume and the visual connection. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Build Your Own Playground – Architecture where no one looks for it]] | — |
| [[The budget is set before the architect arrives]] | Arriving late means the budget was fixed in the previous phase, which removes the room for design entirely.
