| [[The carbon cost abatement curve — free interventions first]] | Ramboll works out cost per kilogram of carbon saved. Early briefing interventions often save cost *and* carbon, so those come first. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Net Zero That Holds Up – Whole-Life Carbon, Design Choices, and Delivery]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]] |
| [[The Cheese Boomerang replaces the Blue Banana]] | The classic Western European logistics corridor is being complemented by an emerging CEE corridor running Poland → Czech Republic → down to Romanian ports, which he calls the "Cheese Boomerang". Power, rail and road investment is following that arc. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Why CEE is the Hottest Location in Europe for supply chain, logistics and industrial Development]] | — |
| [[The commonest mistake is saving carbon too late]] | Teams design as normal, then try to make it low carbon, which forces them to buy best-in-class materials — which is where cost enters. The right day-one question is not "how do we make our materials low carbon" but "how do we make this building low carbon." | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Net Zero That Holds Up – Whole-Life Carbon, Design Choices, and Delivery]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]] |
| [[The competitor is the gaming industry]] | The studio's biggest future competitor is not another practice but the gaming and tech-adjacent industries, which can generate optimised outputs far faster — and logistics centres are named as exactly the typology where automated layout generation will land first. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Henning Larsen Playbook for Biobased Buildings]] | — |
| [[The computer took decades; so will this]] | On the bubble question he refuses the sensationalist read: historically technologies like the computer took decades to seep into economies, so an immediate surge in either productivity or job losses is unlikely; AI's clearer near-term effect is reinforcing the existing rich-north / poorer-south divergence in Europe. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Geography of Growth – Cities, AI, and the future of property]] | — |
| [[The crinkle-crankle wall saves bricks]] | A serpentine garden wall exists to save material: a straight single-wythe wall collapses and needs two lines of bricks, while the zigzag stands with one. The same logic drove Prvok's undulating wall surface — but it only works in two dimensions. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Printing Against Gravity – From the construction site to the research lab]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]] |

| [[The Czech Republic holds ~50% of regional stock — until Poland enters the frame]] | Czechia has roughly 13 million m² of industrial floor space and takes up nearly half the region's stock; Poland alone has 45 million m², roughly equal to all other CEE countries combined, which is why Poland is usually excluded from regional charts. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-disputed">Disputed</span> | [[Why CEE is the Hottest Location in Europe for supply chain, logistics and industrial Development]] | — |
| [[The dearest credits eat sellable floor]] | Two families of expensive credits: artificial technologies with their control, regulation and measuring systems; and anything that consumes space inside the building, because that space cannot be sold or let and is full of technology. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Criteria for a Green Building – Beyond energy performance LEED - BREEAM - WELL]] | — |

| [[The design decision is made 2–3 years before anyone hears of it]] | Network design and strategy takes two to three years before a tenant ever approaches the market. Total project timeline from board idea to operating warehouse is realistically four to seven years. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Why CEE is the Hottest Location in Europe for supply chain, logistics and industrial Development]] | — |
| [[The design outran the supply chain]] | World of Volvo was conceived as a biogenic building that "outperformed" the manufacturing capability then available in Europe; it required an Austrian manufacturer and bespoke digital tools simply to describe the structure. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Plot to Place – Designing Thriving and Resilient Neighborhoods]] | — |
| [[The developer is not a philanthropist — that's the planner's job]] | Asked who ensures coherence when several developers assemble adjoining parks: developers maximise profit, and design quality across a precinct is the responsibility of the city architect, urban planner and government through the master plan. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Why CEE is the Hottest Location in Europe for supply chain, logistics and industrial Development]] | [[Urban integration of logistics buildings]] |
| [[The developer is the interest-aligner]] | Not the architect, not the contractor, not the capital. The function is to reconcile investor, bank, tenant, municipality and community interests — in his words "maybe not compromises", but wins everywhere. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Industrial Architecture as Living Infrastructure – Why future design is defined by systems, data, and adaptability, not just form]] | — |
| [[The developer's job is to push the supply chain]] | Three parts: force new materials through the whole supply chain including small suppliers; engage clients and investors; and advocate for regulatory change — with German and Austrian colleagues lobbying alongside for Czech reform. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Model to Reality – Sustainability in Industrial Buildings]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]] |
| [[The digital Fort Knox]] | Data centres as built are wide, industrial, gated, fenced and access-controlled, with prominent MEP, cooling towers and pipework on roof and flanks. He offers a personal verdict that they are architecturally poor. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Cloud in the city – data centres, urban development and implications]] | [[Visual impact and acceptance by neighbours]], [[Urban integration of logistics buildings]] |
| [[The digital twin lies by 2–5 cm]] | Fresh material deforms as it prints, producing 2–5 cm of deviation barely visible to the eye but fatal to fabrication data: curves exported from Rhino for shelves and the kitchen counter did not fit, and the kitchen desk had to be remade to the as-built curvature measured on site. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Printing Against Gravity – From the construction site to the research lab]] | — |
| [[The dog-walker on the dyke as a design client]] | The woman in Lelystad walking her dog should look at the building and read a generous gesture, not merely Bestseller's profit. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | [[Visual impact and acceptance by neighbours]] |
| [[The Dutch site was ecologically poor to start with]] | The reclaimed Dutch land was artificial, grid-like and near-monocultural — grass plus a hedge of essentially one tree species — so expectations were low; the ecological potential lay in the adjacent shore-water edge zone. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | [[Biodiversity and ecological connectivity on logistics sites]] |
| [[The Dutch state architect said – fewer boxes]] | The Netherlands' College van Rijksadviseurs issued a national advice on "(X)XL verdozing" with four words as its policy: *minder, compacter, geconcentreerder, multifunctioneler* — fewer, more compact, more concentrated, more multifunctional. It called for clustering zones, a ban on greenfield boxes outside them, "red-for-red" relocation of isolated sheds, and mandatory energy generation on roofs and facades. Dutch logistics floorspace grew by roughly 10 million m² in a decade, and seven provinces have since sought to restrain mega-DCs. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-external">External</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[College van Rijksadviseurs, 2019]] | [[Land take of logistics buildings]] |
| [[The early-decision curve applies to water and neighbours too]] | The familiar carbon "decide early" graph generalises: the earliest design decisions govern energy, water and community impacts alike, and once built the building cannot be fixed. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Cloud in the city – data centres, urban development and implications]] | — |
| [[The ecotone is the design brief]] | Where forest meets beach there is a wetland belonging to neither, with higher biodiversity than either — so the zones where two, three or four urban systems collide are the highest-opportunity, highest-complexity design sites. Don't tidy them up; keep the friction. A train station meeting a school should become a station-with-a-school. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Plot to Place – Designing Thriving and Resilient Neighborhoods]] | [[Urban integration of logistics buildings]] |
| [[The electricity mix dominates everything]] | Electricity mix is the single largest determinant of the carbon footprint of everything, buildings included; the EU figure quoted is roughly 8% of total global emissions. Falling EU numbers come from renewables *and* nuclear, which is effectively carbon-free. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Logistics Development in the concept of certification and LCA]] | [[Operational energy and on-site renewables]], [[Operational carbon]] |
| [[The employees are trapped outside the real world]] | The major problem of the out-of-town logistics model is a human one: staff spend their working lives in a place deliberately located outside the city, cut off from what the speakers call the real world. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Build Your Own Playground – Architecture where no one looks for it]] | [[Worker comfort and well-being]], [[Urban integration of logistics buildings]] |
| [[The energy price forecast is always wrong]] | Asked to compute a heat-pump-versus-gas payback, his answer is that no defensible price curve exists: successive official forecasts of oil prices have all missed. The design response is not a better forecast but a solution robust across scenarios. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Model to Reality – Sustainability in Industrial Buildings]] | [[Electricity price]] |
| [[The established digital hubs sit in flood zones]] | Mapping physical climate exposure as a share of local GDP at NUTS-3 level shows several established data centre hubs are relatively exposed — Amsterdam to river and coastal flooding, London along the Thames — while the Nordics are less exposed, adding a resilience argument to their pull. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Geography of Growth – Cities, AI, and the future of property]] | — |
| [[The first taxonomy was too hard for materials]] | He concedes the taxonomy is itself under pressure and being simplified: the first version was excessively demanding, especially on materials, while on energy it is clear, workable and well used by the market. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Model to Reality – Sustainability in Industrial Buildings]] | — |
| [[The flood model was right, and the tenant kept trading]] | Flood risk analysis obtained from the insurer was modelled at concept stage; when the 2024 flood came, the roads around the Kojetín Amazon park went under water almost exactly as modelled, but the facility kept operating. One day of stopped production is a "terrible cost". | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Model to Reality – Sustainability in Industrial Buildings]] | [[Stormwater and sealed surfaces on logistics sites]] |
| [[The green wall on printed concrete died]] | Greenery placed against the printed wall was expected to spread over it but died quickly: in summer sun the concrete becomes extremely hot, killing even plants in pots with water. The irrigation pump also failed and nobody repaired it — maintenance being the big issue with living walls. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Printing Against Gravity – From the construction site to the research lab]] | [[Biodiversity and ecological connectivity on logistics sites]], [[Green plot ratio]] |
| [[The hero-architect sketch is dead]] | Because performance requirements and ecological complexity now exceed any one discipline, the notion of the architect producing a brilliant sketch that becomes the project is obsolete; the team must include anthropologists, ecologists, traffic, light, climate and social-impact specialists. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | — |
| [[The junction paradox – less traffic, no capacity]] | A brownfield redevelopment generating 500 cars where heavy industry once generated 2,000 can still fail its junction test, because through-traffic from newer developments elsewhere absorbed the capacity — leaving the developer to fund a new roundabout. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Industrial Architecture as Living Infrastructure – Why future design is defined by systems, data, and adaptability, not just form]] | — |
| [[The landscape architect arriving last is the old model]] | The failure pattern named explicitly: architects make the building and the landscape architect is called just before completion to make it pretty. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | — |
| [[The largest rammed earth building in Europe is a warehouse]] | Herzog & de Meuron's Ricola Kräuterzentrum in Laufen (2014) is a 111 x 29 x 11 m herb storage and processing hall whose facade is prefabricated rammed earth made from loam, marl and material excavated on site, with tuff-lime mortar layers every eight courses to resist erosion, monolithic and self-supporting against an internal concrete frame. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-external">External</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Herzog & de Meuron, project 369]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]] |
| [[The last node of the warehouse is a room in every building]] | Parcel boxes are already the final point of logistics and only growing; in future every building in the city will contain a room holding that last stage — distributed logistics as urban programme. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Challenge the perception of industrial architecture as 'boxing']] | [[Last-mile delivery emissions and traffic]] |
| [[The lilac vs the oak]] | Lilacs are beautiful, familiar garden plants but non-native and support almost no insects; an oak supports roughly 250 living creatures. What sits "under the iceberg" of a design decision is what actually determines impact. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-disputed">Disputed</span> | [[The Henning Larsen Playbook for Biobased Buildings]] | [[Biodiversity and ecological connectivity on logistics sites]], [[Native species richness]] |
| [[The literal glass wall between collars]] | Offices look directly into the production hall. Acousticians measured noise at an existing Bestseller facility to specify that wall's sound damping, plus vibration mock-ups for structure-borne noise. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | [[Worker comfort and well-being]], [[Noise level]] |
| [[The market is back to its pre-pandemic line]] | Take-up rose ~6% in 2025 (13% year-on-year in H2) after the 2024 low, putting demand back in line with the pre-pandemic five-year average. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Performing Despite the Headwinds – Understanding the drivers of European logistics performance]] | — |
| [[The material bank argument to the client]] | The commercial pitch for disassembly: the building may be worth a lot of money if you deconstruct rather than demolish it and sell off the excess material — the same beams could build sustainable housing. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Challenge the perception of industrial architecture as 'boxing']] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Design for disassembly score]] |
| [[The MHE designed the building, not the architect]] | After winning, the material-handling supplier's design made the formally expressive competition scheme untenable. Expression was displaced to a few "special moments" — the pushed-in entrance, the roof restaurant bump, the office/production seam — likened to the nose of an aircraft varying a plain cylinder. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | [[Automation and operational efficiency of logistics]] |
| [[The middle-sized warehouse is disappearing]] | Current Czech pipeline is barbelled: over 50% of enquiries are for ~1,000 m² cheap space in villages, and simultaneously 10–15 live requirements for 100,000 m²+ automated high-bay monsters. The former sweet spot of 20,000–30,000 m² has thinned out. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Why CEE is the Hottest Location in Europe for supply chain, logistics and industrial Development]] | — |
| [[The money is in logistics; the architecture isn't — that's the niche]] | Warehouses and logistics infrastructure are one of the largest segments of the building market, which means the budgets are there while architectural attention is not. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Build Your Own Playground – Architecture where no one looks for it]] | — |
| [[The office is becoming the amenity that holds the workforce]] | Good developers now build campuses with childcare, gyms and cafés, sharing services across multiple warehouses; a 10,000 m² warehouse typically carries 500–1,000 m² of office and showers. He claims these offices are becoming nicer than city offices. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Why CEE is the Hottest Location in Europe for supply chain, logistics and industrial Development]] | [[Worker comfort and well-being]] |
| [[The organic house shrank to two walls]] | The original design was a free-form living-organism shape; after test prints it was reduced so only the two walls were 3D printed and the top room built in wood. Overhangs at the elliptical window had to be propped by foam layers stacked by hand. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Printing Against Gravity – From the construction site to the research lab]] | — |
| [[The plateau is why siting becomes the design lever]] | Because collective efficiency gains have stalled, the remaining opportunity is locational and systemic. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Cloud in the city – data centres, urban development and implications]] | [[Waste heat and cooling of data centres]] |
| [[The point is an interface, not a shed]] | Closing takeaway: these buildings should not merely house machines and goods but create an interface between machine, people, city, urban fabric and site — succeeding when they become an ecosystem like a district, rather than an isolated utility in a virtual space. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Challenge the perception of industrial architecture as 'boxing']] | — |
| [[The power grievance is half right]] | Communities argue that electricity going into a data centre is electricity not going into the energy transition or EVs; he accepts this as partly valid and partly not, rather than dismissing it. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Cloud in the city – data centres, urban development and implications]] | [[Visual impact and acceptance by neighbours]], [[Social acceptance]] |
| [[The printed material is not concrete]] | What is printed is a cementitious mixture, far more expensive than ordinary concrete, that can solidify within minutes because of accelerant chemistry. The visibly darker top three or four layers are still wet; the greyish ones below have already started gaining strength. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Printing Against Gravity – From the construction site to the research lab]] | — |
| [[The production line length sets the building]] | The plan dimension is fixed by the length of the manufacturing line and the robot that runs it: it cannot easily be turned or rearranged. Two site options (north vs north-east loading) were tested against city connections; each had real downsides. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Challenge the perception of industrial architecture as 'boxing']] | — |
| [[The queue was four times the country]] | Great Britain's grid connection queue had swollen past 700 GW — around four times the capacity the country will need by 2030 — before NESO's 2025 reform reordered it, protecting 283 GW of generation and storage plus 99 GW of transmission-connected demand. In the Netherlands the constraint is already binding: over 14,000 businesses were on the waiting list for a grid connection by October 2025, up from 12,000 months earlier, with the minister conceding "there are no easy solutions anymore." | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-external">External</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[NESO, 2025]] | [[Operational energy and on-site renewables]] |
| [[The rating matters, not the certificate]] | Sale-premium data shows a 2010 LEED building sold at a 16% premium, while in Australia in 2011 a NABERS building scored −7.5%: the rules had moved from a 5-star to a 6-star scale, so buildings holding only three to four stars were penalised while five-star buildings still gained 3%. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Criteria for a Green Building – Beyond energy performance LEED - BREEAM - WELL]] | — |
| [[The real energy breakthrough is building management data]] | Asked about technological breakthroughs, he names building management systems first: developers and landlords are getting good at collecting performance data and tuning buildings to time of day and season, with AI as the next step. A colleague adds that the missing half is knowledge about the people inside. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Why CEE is the Hottest Location in Europe for supply chain, logistics and industrial Development]] | [[Operational energy and on-site renewables]] |
| [[The real green lever is 0.1–0.2 points of interest]] | One incentive that can move developers is financial: banks with deeper ESG policies can offer 0.1 to 0.2 better interest rate, which at these capital volumes is significant. Banks underwrite over 30–40 years, a longer horizon than the investor's — which is why the alignment works. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Build Your Own Playground – Architecture where no one looks for it]] | — |
| [[The reduction roadmap led to the activism]] | The reasoning came from the data in a Danish reduction roadmap: emissions cannot be reduced by building new, only by using what already exists. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Henning Larsen Playbook for Biobased Buildings]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Embodied carbon saved by reuse]] |
| [[The region is treated as one product, not fifteen countries]] | EU membership, Schengen expansion and multi-country developer networks reduce friction, so a tenant can replicate an identical building from Czechia to Romania or Slovenia. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Why CEE is the Hottest Location in Europe for supply chain, logistics and industrial Development]] | — |
| [[The right answer is three materials, not one]] | On a Copenhagen office, seventeen floor solutions were tested against weight, structural depth and carbon. Timber won on weight and carbon but lost on depth. The built answer used three systems in different zones. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Net Zero That Holds Up – Whole-Life Carbon, Design Choices, and Delivery]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Embodied carbon intensity]] |
| [[The risk model runs on ordinary architectural parameters]] | The inputs the diagnosis wants are the designer's own variables: cooling system, energy consumption, heating type, the nearby surroundings and whether the building is attached to others, its shape, its façade colour, its window-to-wall ratio. An architect can fill the form "in a few minutes" because they already hold the data. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Beyond the Concrete – Designing Buildings That Last]] | — |
| [[The roof as employee ground]] | Roof use need not be solar or green roof: on the factory they proposed a basketball court and a beehive. What limits a usable roof is worth asking rather than assuming. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Challenge the perception of industrial architecture as 'boxing']] | [[Worker comfort and well-being]] |
| [[The same firm adopts AI differently in a city]] | There is a consistent "urban premium" in AI usage, and part of it is a pure location effect: a law or consulting firm inside a city is more likely to use AI than the same kind of firm in a peripheral area, because urban firms compete globally. ICT shows almost no location effect. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Geography of Growth – Cities, AI, and the future of property]] | — |
| [[The sewage plant as the city's best picnic spot]] | A wastewater treatment facility in Aarhus was designed as an inhabited landscape rather than an excluded utility — landscape laid over the treatment works, turned into a learning space and public destination. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Henning Larsen Playbook for Biobased Buildings]] | [[Public access and community use of logistics sites]], [[Visual impact and acceptance by neighbours]] |
| [[The shoebox is too crude for eye comfort]] | Parametric shoebox models are adequate for trading window area against thermal load, but not for eyesight comfort: they omit the window sill, wall thickness and window frame, and modelling at the fidelity glare analysis needs is too tedious to run parametrically. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Criteria for a Green Building – Beyond energy performance LEED - BREEAM - WELL]] | [[Worker comfort and well-being]] |
| [[The shop becomes a warehouse, the warehouse becomes a shop]] | E-commerce proximity speeds delivery but weakens the shops that make a city centre feel alive. He speculates that the two could converge: the shop becoming logistics, or logistics becoming the shop. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | [[Urban integration of logistics buildings]], [[Adam Večeřa]] |
| [[The sky is brightening 10% a year]] | Analysis of more than 50,000 citizen-science observations from 2011–2022 found night sky brightness increasing by 9.6% per year, nearly five times faster than satellites detect. A child born where 250 stars were visible will see about 100 by the time they turn 18. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-external">External</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Kyba et al., Science, 2023, via NOIRLab]] | — |
| [[The Spanish paradox – more workers, flat incomes]] | Spanish cities with very strong population and employment growth have seen weak productivity and stagnating household incomes — headline growth masking a per-household standstill. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Geography of Growth – Cities, AI, and the future of property]] | — |
| [[The stranded gas station as a design brief]] | Asked for a transition-risk example, they describe (deliberately unnamed) an operator of numerous filling stations who knows electrification will remove demand for gasoline, and is now redesigning the asset's revenue model: EV charging plus something to entertain people during the charging wait. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Beyond the Concrete – Designing Buildings That Last]] | [[Adaptable and relocatable logistics structures]] |
| [[The UK model – pay, and they must deliver]] | In the UK a residential zone triggers a costed list of schools, bus stops and rail connections funded by a developer contribution, and the municipality is legally obliged to deliver or refund. In CZ the developer pays a contribution the city spends elsewhere — and still builds its own infrastructure. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Industrial Architecture as Living Infrastructure – Why future design is defined by systems, data, and adaptability, not just form]] | — |
| [[The whole point is being in business in 20 years]] | To ensure that they are still in business in five, ten, twenty years and not just surviving but thriving. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Beyond the Concrete – Designing Buildings That Last]] | — |
| [[They don't forecast AI at all]] | Oxford Economics does not formally forecast AI growth because of data reliability. Instead they proxy it: sector mix, workforce education levels, willingness to return to education as an upskilling proxy, and R&D/investment flows by city. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Geography of Growth – Cities, AI, and the future of property]] | — |
| [[They tested it on their own office and found flood risk]] | The demonstration building is EY's own Prague office; the diagnosis returns heat, rainfall and floods as its main climate risks, though existing adaptation measures pull the vulnerability score below the exposure score. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Beyond the Concrete – Designing Buildings That Last]] | — |
| [[Thirty-metre freezers are a heat source]] | In the food factory the 30 m freezers and chillers produce so much excess heat that it can heat the parts of the building where people are concentrated. "Once you do things like this, it's no brainer" — what you need is interconnectivity. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Challenge the perception of industrial architecture as 'boxing']] | [[Waste heat recovery]] |
| [[Thixotropy, and why you hammer the hose]] | The mix behaves opposite to ordinary intuition: agitated it flows like honey, at rest it stands solid, which is exactly what lets a fresh layer hold its shape before the chemical reaction starts. It is also why crews hammer the hose — added vibration re-liquefies material that has stiffened during a pause of as little as five minutes. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Printing Against Gravity – From the construction site to the research lab]] | — |
| [[Three failure families, one weakest link]] | Prvok exposed three groups of problems: the containment/stair-stepping mismatch between sliced object and digital model, early-age deformation and collapse, and anisotropy — strong within a layer, weak between layers, so cracks occur at layer interfaces. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Printing Against Gravity – From the construction site to the research lab]] | — |
| [[Three price scenarios, not one number]] | Asked how to plan under energy price uncertainty: a low-risk baseline, a middle case, and a high-risk case — three price and conversion scenarios. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Model to Reality – Sustainability in Industrial Buildings]] | [[Electricity price]] |
| [[Three tiers of ambition on a plot]] | An implicit ladder: the default is bulldoze and build; better is to avoid net loss (keep the trees); best is a measured biodiversity net gain. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | [[Biodiversity and ecological connectivity on logistics sites]], [[Biodiversity net gain]] |
| [[Three years and 3,000 people, not a drawing board]] | Against the normal masterplanning method, Downsview took three years of work with over 3,000 residents to "massage" the winning competition proposal into something robust. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Plot to Place – Designing Thriving and Resilient Neighborhoods]] | — |
| [[Timber buildings overheat without thermal mass]] | Losing concrete's night-cooled thermal mass means timber buildings show overheating; designers must be far more careful about glazing ratios. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Henning Larsen Playbook for Biobased Buildings]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Thermal comfort]] |
| [[Timber is scarce — spend it displacing steel and concrete]] | Timber's value is displacement, not maximisation. There is not enough of it today to displace those materials at scale, so it should be used sparingly and precisely. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Net Zero That Holds Up – Whole-Life Carbon, Design Choices, and Delivery]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]] |
| [[Timber sells on schedule, not on carbon]] | The business case is time and money: price roughly the same as the standard build, one month saved, so revenue from the client's core business starts earlier; simple repeatable shapes remove costly detailing; mistakes are cheaper to fix. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Build Your Own Playground – Architecture where no one looks for it]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Construction cost]] |
| [[Timber turns 60,000 t CO2 into a surplus — conditionally]] | Viewed "in carbon goggles", the conventional version of the timber district would embody almost 60,000 tonnes of CO2; in timber it shows a surplus of carbon sequestration — explicitly conditional on the timber not being burned after 50 years, with EU legislation expected to support that accounting. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Plot to Place – Designing Thriving and Resilient Neighborhoods]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Embodied carbon intensity]], [[Life-cycle impact by stage]] |
| [[Timber, brick and light concrete came out near-identical]] | One house designed in three material variants (Porotherm, autoclaved light concrete, timber) showed only very small differences in total emissions, because the foundations dominated. The timber structure itself was lower-impact, but that advantage disappeared in the total. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Logistics Development in the concept of certification and LCA]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Embodied carbon intensity]] |
| [[Today's sheds are tomorrow's convertible stock]] | The generic modern box — 10–12 m clear height, good column grid, flat floors — is the frame that converted well in Western Europe, and is expected to absorb data centres quickly. City-swallowed older industrial in Prague is already becoming fitness centres, basketball courts and retail parks. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Industrial Architecture as Living Infrastructure – Why future design is defined by systems, data, and adaptability, not just form]] | [[Adaptable and relocatable logistics structures]], [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Adaptability]] |
| [[Today's waste stream should be tomorrow's solved problem]] | Building with a waste stream (such as fibreboard from construction) should be temporary: if the waste problem is solved, in five years the recycled material should come from a different stream. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | — |
| [[Toolchain as hiring criterion]] | Henning Larsen will not employ architects who work in SketchUp; Rhino/Grasshopper is mandatory because the tool is the shared language across a global studio. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Henning Larsen Playbook for Biobased Buildings]] | — |
| [[Topology optimisation isn't buildable yet — use waffles meanwhile]] | On 3D-printed and topologically optimised slabs (Block Research Group name-checked), the lab-to-site gap is real. Intermediate steps are waffle slabs, cast void-forming, then more sophisticated tessellated precast. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Net Zero That Holds Up – Whole-Life Carbon, Design Choices, and Delivery]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]] |
| [[Traffic engineers are expert space-consumers]] | Green corridors are a fight over surface area: since no new space exists in cities, corridors mean reducing paved surface and increasing permeable softscape, which simultaneously solves rainwater. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | [[Stormwater and sealed surfaces on logistics sites]], [[Impervious surface ratio]] |
| [[Transparency – 600 PDF pages versus an open tool]] | DGNB's methodology is public but arrives as roughly 600 pages of PDF; BREEAM's checklist is findable with effort; LEED's is essentially gated behind registration. SBTool CZ is free and online, has run for 15 years, and adds a generic typology by end of this year. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Logistics Development in the concept of certification and LCA]] | — |
| [[Transport is half the supply-chain cost, rent a tenth]] | For a standard ambient distribution warehouse, roughly 50% of supply-chain cost is transport, 20–30% labour, and only 5–10% rent. This inverts the usual real-estate framing. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Why CEE is the Hottest Location in Europe for supply chain, logistics and industrial Development]] | — |
| [[Tuning a building takes two years]] | Getting a finished building into a genuinely efficient operating state can take two years, and it is always mediated by the people and the company that occupy it. Commissioning is not an event. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Criteria for a Green Building – Beyond energy performance LEED - BREEAM - WELL]] | [[Operational energy and on-site renewables]] |
| [[Twelve months from signature to handover]] | Site, general contractor and basic concept are all ready before the client is signed, but the entire construction must be delivered within 12 months — and the concept still has to be reworked in the first few months around a client technology the client itself cannot yet specify. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Model to Reality – Sustainability in Industrial Buildings]] | — |
| [[Two Czech databases disagree by 10%+]] | Comparing One Click LCA's data against the Czech data.LCA.cz database gives differences of 10% and more — even using the datasets One Click LCA itself labels as representative for Czechia. Using Danish data, with its very green energy mix, produces completely different numbers and limits. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Logistics Development in the concept of certification and LCA]] | [[Embodied carbon intensity]] |
| [[Two engines, or growth goes wrong]] | Population growth and productivity growth solve different problems and must run together; with only population you get congestion and stagnating household incomes, with only productivity you get an efficient, ageing, slow-growing city. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Geography of Growth – Cities, AI, and the future of property]] | — |
| [[Two fewer sick days pays for the building]] | Citing certified-building data, workers are typically sick two days less per year in certified buildings; those two days of inactivity per employee are what actually makes the business case, not kilowatt-hours. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Criteria for a Green Building – Beyond energy performance LEED - BREEAM - WELL]] | [[Worker comfort and well-being]], [[Worker well-being]] |
| [[Two knobs decide everything – scenario and horizon]] | Every climate risk result is a function of exactly two choices — which IPCC scenario and which time horizon. RCP is explained as watts per square metre of additional forcing; RCP 8.5 is stated as 8.5 W/m² with a warming range around 3.2–5.4°C. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Beyond the Concrete – Designing Buildings That Last]] | — |
| [[Two pilots contradict each other on batteries]] | Battery swapping and on-street static charging are presented as competing answers to the same question, with swapping carrying its own operational cost and extra service-vehicle trips; the on-street charging pilot in Norway, Finland and Sweden showed a 50–70% reduction in battery swapping and fewer service trips. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Integrated Urban Logistics – From last-mile to mobility hubs]] | — |
| [[Two specified layers failed on site]] | The interior clay board proved too fragile and not humidity-resistant during installation and was swapped for plaster board; the wood-fibre wind stopper was replaced with a Class A non-combustible weather board to give a fire separation between rain screen and straw element. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | — |
| [[Two storeys because there was no third site]] | SEGRO and IKEA's Paris Air² Logistique at the Port of Gennevilliers is 63,000 m² over two storeys, built vertically because it occupied the last large plot available in the port. BREEAM Excellent, river-rail-road connected, serving Parisian stores. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-external">External</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[SEGRO, 2019]] | [[Land take of logistics buildings]] |
| [[Two thirds of energy is lost as heat]] | About two thirds of all energy in current US and European systems is lost as heat, so electrifying raises end efficiency — the reason heat pumps and PV win. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Model to Reality – Sustainability in Industrial Buildings]] | — |
| [[Two twin harbours, opposite performance]] | North Harbour (Århusgade) and South Harbour (Sluseholmen) are near-identical in location, size, age and typology, yet the higher placemaking score tracks higher average price per m², lower reported loneliness, and far more visitors in the flow data. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Plot to Place – Designing Thriving and Resilient Neighborhoods]] | [[Social interaction index]] |
| [[Under-construction volumes down ~40% from peak]] | Space under construction has fallen roughly 40% from the 2022 peak, with declines in Italy, Netherlands, France, UK and Germany. Completions fell ~7% in 2025 to roughly 15–16 million m². | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Performing Despite the Headwinds – Understanding the drivers of European logistics performance]] | — |
| [[Utah's random giants are not the model]] | Prefacing that this is a personal view and not his company's: AI-serving data centres can be sited anywhere, and in the US go wherever land is cheap and building is easy — producing very large projects in "completely random" places. He is "really not a big fan" of that model. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Cloud in the city – data centres, urban development and implications]] | [[Urban integration of logistics buildings]] |
| [[Vacancy – 5.5% average, but zero in Munich and Hamburg]] | European logistics vacancy averages ~5.5%. Slovakia, Poland and Spain run high; the UK jumped from 2.5% to 7%; Germany stays very low, and Munich and Hamburg have "literally no vacancy," which drives their rental growth. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Performing Despite the Headwinds – Understanding the drivers of European logistics performance]] | — |
| [[Vertical zoning is the answer to people-meet-machines]] | Where public and logistics collide, stack them: the ground floor is strictly logistics and industry, and the public route runs above on an elevated skywalk. Safety first, without compromising logistics efficiency. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Challenge the perception of industrial architecture as 'boxing']] | [[Pedestrian–truck safety on logistics sites]], [[Pedestrian–vehicle conflict points]], [[Khrystyna Verbitska]] |
| [[Volvo's roof belongs to everyone]] | In Sweden, allemansrätten (right to roam) means nature cannot be owned: the World of Volvo site is Volvo's, but no ticket is required to walk the roof and surrounding landscape. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Henning Larsen Playbook for Biobased Buildings]] | [[Public access and community use of logistics sites]] |
| [[Warehouse belts create heat islands between fields]] | Logistics clusters form large heat islands, and because they sit among agricultural fields, the heat island compounds drought problems in the surrounding landscape. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Logistics Development in the concept of certification and LCA]] | [[Outdoor microclimate around logistics buildings]], [[Urban heat island effect]] |
| [[Warehouse demand weak; data centres and defence carry it]] | For Prague and CEE he expects weak near-term industrial and warehousing demand, with German weakness spilling over. The offsetting drivers: data-centre construction and servicing logistics, and — more speculatively — Germany's planned fiscal and defence spending creating demand for defence facilities and related warehousing. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Geography of Growth – Cities, AI, and the future of property]] | — |
| [[Warehouses aren't sexy — but that's changing]] | He opens by conceding architects' likely prejudice, then counters with multi-storey warehouses in Hamburg and GLP's sky-blending facades. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Performing Despite the Headwinds – Understanding the drivers of European logistics performance]] | [[Visual impact and acceptance by neighbours]] |
| [[Warehouses break the user-comfort premise]] | Certification tools are still built around a human user. In a highly automated hall with robots and almost nobody inside, user-comfort criteria have no subject — named as a fundamental limitation for this typology. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Logistics Development in the concept of certification and LCA]] | [[Automation and operational efficiency of logistics]] |
| [[Warming has social consequences that are also risks]] | SSPs are introduced as the second scenario family, on the argument that a warmer environment produces socio-economic consequences — migration, higher rates of illness — which a complete asset-level assessment has to cover. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Beyond the Concrete – Designing Buildings That Last]] | — |
| [[Waste heat is already an approval condition]] | In parts of Europe, particularly the north, heat recovery into district heating is now a routine design and planning consideration every time a data centre is submitted for approval — homes, offices, swimming pools. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Cloud in the city – data centres, urban development and implications]] | [[Waste heat and cooling of data centres]], [[Waste heat recovery]] |
| [[Waste is the segment cities actually control]] | Unlike other segments, waste flows are predictable and publicly controlled, which makes them a strong entry point for electrification, data-driven optimisation and system-wide innovation — and ties directly to urban design decisions such as bin placement, access and street layout. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Integrated Urban Logistics – From last-mile to mobility hubs]] | — |
| [[We assign a moral price to materials]] | The barrier to reuse is perceptual as much as technical: we look at old artwork and old construction elements with awe, but dismiss recently reclaimed material. "The older it is, the higher the value in our perception" — while young reuse material is dismissed. In the Netherlands, reclaimed piles are considered *higher* aesthetic quality than new ones. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Challenge the perception of industrial architecture as 'boxing']] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]] |
| [[We can price carbon, not nature]] | The profession has become good at calculating carbon and can now calculate biodiversity net gain, but has no way to value air cleaning, stormwater handling or community wellbeing — so nature has no monetary standing in the argument. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Nature positive by design – Creating lasting impact by designing for and with nature]] | [[Social cost of carbon]] |
| [[We had no criteria, so we borrowed Gehl's]] | To score placemaking they had to adopt Jan Gehl's 12 criteria, because no other benchmark existed in academia or anywhere else. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Plot to Place – Designing Thriving and Resilient Neighborhoods]] | — |
| [[We killed our own business there]] | After seven years with Penny, YUAR produced a design manual allowing the chain to replicate the store nationally without them. Roughly 80% of the documentation can be identical, but the authorisation of each permit must still be unique — which is the residual fee. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Build Your Own Playground – Architecture where no one looks for it]] | — |
| [[We planned people for decades; goods got none of it]] | We spent decades planning how people move. And now we really need to plan how goods are moving around our cities with the same level of attention. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Integrated Urban Logistics – From last-mile to mobility hubs]] | — |
| [[Weights should change with typology]] | In SBTool CZ the weighting differs per building type: for administrative buildings energy, drinking water and LCA dominate; for schools the heaviest criteria are indoor environmental quality and interiors. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Logistics Development in the concept of certification and LCA]] | — |
| [[WELL is the one that waits]] | LEED and BREEAM are assessed through design and construction and effectively close at commissioning; WELL is aimed at the operational state, at how the building affects its occupants once running. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Criteria for a Green Building – Beyond energy performance LEED - BREEAM - WELL]] | — |
| [[Wetlands chosen partly as carbon sinks]] | Analysis showed wetlands sequester the most carbon of any landscape type, making the retention wetland a carbon strategy as well as a stormwater and biodiversity one. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Big Box, Small Footprint – Lessons from Bestseller's Low-Carbon Logistics Campus]] | [[Carbon sequestration by planting]] |
| [[What is sold as 3D printing is 2.5D]] | The promise that printing lets you build anything is not met: most projects draw a plan and extrude it upward, with no overhangs or openings — stacking identical layers, properly called 2.5D printing. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Printing Against Gravity – From the construction site to the research lab]] | — |
| [[What performs is what you can't see]] | The photogenic surface versus the invisible performance: acoustics solved selectively (enclosed meeting rooms, plus porous unchipped brick left raw because it absorbs more sound than a flat wall), and above all layout and exposure. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Build Your Own Playground – Architecture where no one looks for it]] | [[Worker comfort and well-being]] |
| [[What will we do if we replace every job by a robot]] | Left open as a social question, after noting that a facility went fully automated because human error had cost the company large sums. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Build Your Own Playground – Architecture where no one looks for it]] | [[Automation and operational efficiency of logistics]], [[Jobs created]] |
| [[White facades, urban heat and the wind that saves them]] | Uncertain about reflection and urban heat risk from their standard white facade, the team ran microclimate simulations with external experts; wind, building orientation and surrounding greenery together proved sufficient to manage the heat and glare risk. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Model to Reality – Sustainability in Industrial Buildings]] | [[Outdoor microclimate around logistics buildings]], [[Urban heat island effect]] |
| [[Whole-life carbon usually isn't whole]] | Lifecycle modules follow EN 15978 (A upfront, B in use, C end of life, D beyond). C is typically under 10%; A is typically one third to 50%. People routinely say "whole life carbon" while silently excluding modules, and national regulations differ on which. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Net Zero That Holds Up – Whole-Life Carbon, Design Choices, and Delivery]] | [[Life-cycle impact by stage]], [[Whole-life carbon]] |
| [[Why CEE – demand, workers, cost — in that order]] | A 2025 KPMG survey of ~130 companies found local consumer demand the top reason for choosing CEE, followed by availability of qualified workers, then low labour cost; 22% of German respondents cited ease of doing business. He notes the region's rare "sweet spot" of university-educated technical staff alongside a deep blue-collar workforce. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Why CEE is the Hottest Location in Europe for supply chain, logistics and industrial Development]] | — |
| [[Wind in Czechia is a mentality problem]] | Pressed on why wind generation next to a warehouse is normal in the Netherlands and effectively impossible in Czechia, he declines to blame regulation alone: master planning matters, but so does the mentality of the region — the same pattern he sees in slow EV uptake. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Model to Reality – Sustainability in Industrial Buildings]] | — |
| [[Wire reinforcement doesn't bond like rebar]] | Groups in the Netherlands deposit steel wire directly into the layers, but a smooth wire embedded in this mixture, without proper compaction around it, does not bond as well as reinforcement in conventional concrete — where steel and concrete must "become very good friends." | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Printing Against Gravity – From the construction site to the research lab]] | — |
| [[Yields – 8% then 3.5% then 5%, now flat]] | Logistics yields fell from ~8% in 2009–10 to a low of ~3.5%, corrected sharply during the rate rises to ~5%, and have levelled at about 5.25% — roughly in line with retail, while office yields have begun compressing again. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Performing Despite the Headwinds – Understanding the drivers of European logistics performance]] | — |
| [[You can buy a second-hand warehouse]] | A UK website sells used buildings; because logistics buildings are highly standardised, buying one — or several — and recombining them behind a new facade would save a massive amount of carbon. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Net Zero That Holds Up – Whole-Life Carbon, Design Choices, and Delivery]] | [[Embodied carbon and adaptive reuse of logistics buildings]], [[Adaptable and relocatable logistics structures]], [[Embodied carbon saved by reuse]] |
| [[You can have a dog, but not a cat]] | Buying an apartment at Fælledby means signing up to keep only indoor cats, because free-roaming cats predate the small birds the biodiversity strategy is built around. Nature accessible to everyone, owned by no one. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[The Henning Larsen Playbook for Biobased Buildings]] | — |
| [[You can model anything; the client is the unknown]] | Modelling capability is not the constraint — understanding how the occupier behaves inside the building is. Panattoni calibrates models using operating data from a comparable building the same client runs in another country. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[From Model to Reality – Sustainability in Industrial Buildings]] | [[Operational energy and on-site renewables]] |
| [[You can't reinvent the warehouse any more than the container]] | Warehouse form is treated as effectively settled, for the same reason container dimensions are: it is standardised to a worldwide system. Innovation therefore has to happen in operation and sharing, not in shape. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Build Your Own Playground – Architecture where no one looks for it]] | — |
| [[Your professional emissions dwarf your personal ones]] | Personal footprints in the UK/EU run about 5–8 tonnes per year; the emissions a designer influences professionally are enormous by comparison, and architects have the largest influence of all. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Net Zero That Holds Up – Whole-Life Carbon, Design Choices, and Delivery]] | — |
| [[Your site decides a whole credit block]] | Transport credits depend on the site: with no metro, tram or bus you cannot earn a single point, and with no cycle route passing the site you cannot claim bicycle facilities however good the internal provision. The fallback is earning at least one credit for green vehicles. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Criteria for a Green Building – Beyond energy performance LEED - BREEAM - WELL]] | [[Urban integration of logistics buildings]], [[Public transport accessibility]] |
| [[Zero incentive for greenery in logistics]] | Asked directly how strong the incentive is for developers to add trees and green space to Czech logistics parks, the answer is flatly negative for logistics — while the same incentive is growing for offices, because offices must now compete to pull people back in. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Build Your Own Playground – Architecture where no one looks for it]] | [[Biodiversity and ecological connectivity on logistics sites]] |
| [[Zoning permits you to apply, not to build]] | A Czech master plan grants no timing certainty and no infrastructure capacity. Developers must buy and permit land years before any customer exists: up to 10 years in Prague, 1.5–5 years in regional towns. | <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--source rl-badge--source-expert">Expert</span> <span class="rl-badge rl-badge--evidence rl-badge--evidence-open-for-review">Open for expert review</span> | [[Industrial Architecture as Living Infrastructure – Why future design is defined by systems, data, and adaptability, not just form]] | — |
